{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"6d6f6da1-0dda-49ba-98c2-ecb094db874a","agentId":"a08348e19783bf157","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Add the operational/deployment layer for SIGIL — the \"LOW ops\" production findings (no systemd units, no runbook, no log rotation, no backup procedure for the irreplaceable spine). Repo /home/kali/sigil, branch `prod-tier2` (shared checkout). RULES: (1) create ONLY NEW files under a new `deploy/` directory + a `RUNBOOK.md` at repo root — do NOT edit any existing `sigil/` code, `pyproject.toml`, or `.github/`; (2) DO NOT run ANY git command; leave changes UNCOMMITTED. This is docs + config templates, no Python behavior change.\n\nCONTEXT (read to be accurate, don't edit): `sigil/cli.py` (the real subcommands: ingest, index, sign, search, graph, consolidate, agents, voice, warden, audit, dashboard, verify, status, serve, host, mesh, bridge, scrape, doctor), `sigil/config.py` (SIGIL_HOME=~/.sigil holds spine/keys/vectors/graph 0700; env `SIGIL_HOME`, `SIGIL_LOG_LEVEL`, `SIGIL_KERNEL_BIN`, `SIGIL_CLAUDE_BIN`, `SIGIL_INGEST_REPOS`), `sigil/bridge/server.py` (the `sigil bridge serve --addr <wg-ip>` daemon with SIGTERM graceful shutdown), `sigil/mcp/server.py` (the stdio MCP memory server), `README.md`, `demo/README.md`.\n\nCREATE:\n\n1. `deploy/systemd/sigil-bridge.service` — a systemd unit (user or system, pick user-level `~/.config/systemd/user/` style and document it) that runs `sigil bridge serve --addr %i` (or an EnvironmentFile-driven addr) with: `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec`, `Environment=SIGIL_LOG_LEVEL=INFO`, an `EnvironmentFile=%h/.sigil/bridge.env` for `SIGIL_HOME`/addr/port, `ExecStart` using the installed `sigil` console command, hardening directives that DON'T break it (`NoNewPrivileges=yes`, `ProtectSystem=strict`, `ReadWritePaths=%h/.sigil`, `PrivateTmp=yes`), and `KillSignal=SIGTERM` + `TimeoutStopSec` (the server handles SIGTERM gracefully). Add a header comment: pin the WireGuard IP, never 0.0.0.0.\n\n2. `deploy/systemd/sigil-consolidate.timer` + `deploy/systemd/sigil-consolidate.service` — a systemd timer running the nightly `sigil consolidate --provider heuristic` (offline, no spend) at a low-traffic hour, oneshot, with the same hardening + EnvironmentFile.\n\n3. `deploy/logrotate/sigil` — a logrotate config template for SIGIL logs IF SIGIL logs to a file (note: SIGIL logs to stderr by default via `obs.configure_logging`; under systemd that goes to the journal, so document that journald handles rotation and this file is for the case where the operator redirects stderr to `~/.sigil/logs/*.log`). Weekly, rotate, compress, size cap, `copytruncate`.\n\n4. `RUNBOOK.md` (repo root) — a concise operator runbook:\n   - **Install** (link the README; `pip install -e .` / `.[all]`, build the kernel, register MCP, `sigil doctor`).\n   - **Run the daemons** (bridge via systemd unit above; MCP server via Claude registration; how to enable/start/stop/status the units: `systemctl --user enable --now sigil-bridge@<wg-ip>` etc; `journalctl --user -u sigil-bridge -f` for logs).\n   - **Self-check**: `sigil doctor` (what each check means; what to do when a check fails — keyring absent, kernel missing, Qdrant down).\n   - **Backup & restore** (CRITICAL — the spine + keys are IRREPLACEABLE): what to back up (`~/.sigil/spine/`, `~/.sigil/keys/`, `~/.sigil/head.json`), how (a simple tar/rsync of `~/.sigil/` while quiescent; note the spine is append-only + hash-chained so a consistent snapshot is a plain file copy; `sigil verify`/integrity to validate a restore), and the 0700/0600 permission requirement on restore. Warn that losing the owner Ed25519 key means losing the ability to sign approvals/head.\n   - **Upgrade** (git pull, `pip install -e .`, rebuild the kernel, re-run `sigil doctor`, run the test suite).\n   - **Troubleshooting** table (symptom → check → fix): bridge won't bind (bind_ok refuses public — use the WG/Tailscale IP), oracle/kernel absent (build it / set SIGIL_KERNEL_BIN), Qdrant down (`docker start sigil-qdrant`), a torn spine tail (reads self-recover; `sigil verify` confirms), phone can't pair (fingerprint mismatch → re-check).\n   - **Log levels & observability**: `SIGIL_LOG_LEVEL`, journald, what is NEVER logged (secrets/tokens/full pubkeys — only prefixes).\n\nBe accurate to the actual CLI + config (don't invent commands/flags — verify against cli.py). Keep it concise + operator-focused. Do NOT run git. Report the files you created and any assumption you made. Leave UNCOMMITTED."},"uuid":"b9c3de32-be06-4eca-88f1-7330edc7a7bf","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:35:49.310Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Use it when a task names CRUCIBLE, OBSIDIAN, `python3 -m framework.v2 <subcommand>`, findings/oracles/evidence certificates, the blackboard event spine, critics, reflection, or the reward/calibration loop.\n- deep-research: Deep research harness — fan-out web searches, fetch sources, adversarially verify claims, synthesize a cited report. - When the user wants a deep, multi-source, fact-checked research report on any topic. BEFORE invoking, check if the question is specific enough to research directly — if underspecified (e.g., \"what car to buy\" without budget/use-case/region), ask 2-3 clarifying questions to narrow scope. Then pass the refined question as args, weaving the answers in.\n- adversarial-sweep: Independent multi-lens adversarial re-attack of a defensive-oracle domain — the FORGE stage-9 companion to RED-PEN. Attack from disjoint lenses, refute each objection with a real repro, report only what survives. - Before merging any AEGIS/FORGE domain stream, alongside (not instead of) the red-pen agent. Pass the domain spec via args. On the email-auth stream it caught 3 CRITICAL/LOW defects PAST a RED-PEN PASS — near-zero-FP cannot be self-certified by one reviewer.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. \"SPINE_PATH\": str(spine / \"spine.jsonl\"),\n148\t        \"HEAD_PATH\": str(spine / \"head.json\"),\n149\t        \"KEYS_DIR\": str(spine / \"keys\"),\n150\t        \"CACHE_DIR\": str(home / \"cache\"),\n151\t        \"QDRANT_PATH\": str(home / \"qdrant\"),\n152\t        \"GRAPH_DIR\": str(home / \"graph\"),\n153\t        \"CLAUDE_PROJECTS\": os.environ.get(\"SIGIL_CLAUDE_PROJECTS\", os.environ.get(\"SIGIL_EMBED_MODEL\", \"BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5\"),\n158\t        \"SCOPE\": os.environ.get(\"SIGIL_SCOPE\", \"sigil\"),\n159\t        \"OWNER_KEY_ID\": os.environ.get(\"SIGIL_OWNER_KEY_ID\", \"owner\"),\n160\t        \"CLAUDE_BIN\": claude_bin(),\n161\t        \"KERNEL_BIN\": kernel_bin(),\n162\t        \"INGEST_REPOS\": v in cfg.items()}\n167\t\n168\t\n169\tdef _probe_writable(d: Path) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n170\t    try:\n171\t        d.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n172\t        probe = d / \".sigil-doctor-probe\"\n173\t        probe.write_text(\"ok\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n174\t        probe.unlink()\n175\t        return True, f\"{d} writable\"\n176\t    except OSError as e:\n177\t        return False, f\"{d} NOT writable: {e}\"\n178\t\n179\t\n180\tdef _check_kernel() -> tuple[str, bool, str]:\n181\t    kb = kernel_bin()\n182\t    return (\"kernel_binary\", kb is not None,\n183\t _check_qdrant() -> tuple[str, bool, str]:\n193\t    url = os.environ.get(\"SIGIL_QDRANT_URL\", \"\")\n194\t    if not url:\n195\t        return (\"qdrant\", True, f\"embedded/local-mode at {"parentUuid":"3ba436dd-5c03-4024-84d8-9a934ac3869b","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"6d6f6da1-0dda-49ba-98c2-ecb094db874a","agentId":"a08348e19783bf157","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_011R9c2wPtLxz18vYVfTuTDZ","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Structured logging for SIGIL (production-hardening OBS).\n2\t\n3\tApp entry points (the CLI, daemons) call `configure_logging()` ONCE at startup to install a\n4\tsingle root handler; library modules just use `logging.getLogger(__name__)` (or `get_logger`)\n5\tand inherit that handler. Logs go to stderr so they never pollute stdout / CLI data output.\n6\t\n7\tDiscipline: a log line is diagnostic, never a secret sink. NEVER pass an API key, token, or any\n8\tcredential to a logger — this module deliberately provides no \"log everything\" helper, and\n9\t`config.effective_config()` is the redacted way to surface configuration.\n10\t\"\"\"\n11\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n12\t\n13\timport logging\n14\timport os\n15\timport sys\n16\t\n17\t_HANDLER_NAME = \"sigil\"\n18\t_DEFAULT_LEVEL = \"INFO\"\n19\t_FORMAT = \"%(asctime)s %(levelname)-7s %(name)s: %(message)s\"\n20\t_DATEFMT = \"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S\"\n21\t_configured = False\n22\t\n23\t\n24\tdef _resolve_level(level=None) -> int:\n25\t    \"\"\"Level from explicit arg → env SIGIL_LOG_LEVEL → INFO. Accepts an int or a name\n26\t    ('DEBUG'/'info'/…); an unknown name falls back to INFO rather than raising.\"\"\"\n27\t    raw = level if level is not None else os.environ.get(\"SIGIL_LOG_LEVEL\", _DEFAULT_LEVEL)\n28\t    if isinstance(raw, int):\n29\t        return raw\n30\t    return getattr(logging, str(raw).strip().upper(), logging.INFO)\n31\t\n32\t\n33\tdef configure_logging(level=None, *, stream=None, force: bool = False) -> logging.Logger:\n34\t    \"\"\"Install SIGIL's single root log handler and return the root logger. Idempotent: a second\n35\t    call only adjusts the level unless `force=True` (which rebuilds the handler — used by tests to\n36\t    capture into a StringIO). Concise timestamped format; writes to `stream` (default stderr).\"\"\"\n37\t    global _configured\n38\t    root = logging.getLogger()\n39\t    lvl = _resolve_level(level)\n40\t    if _configured and not force:\n41\t        root.setLevel(lvl)\n42\t        return root\n43\t    # drop any handler we previously installed so re-configuring never double-emits\n44\t    for h in list(root.handlers):\n45\t        if getattr(h, \"name\", None) == _HANDLER_NAME:\n46\t            root.removeHandler(h)\n47\t    handler = logging.StreamHandler(stream or sys.stderr)\n48\t    handler.set_name(_HANDLER_NAME)\n49\t    handler.setFormatter(logging.Formatter(_FORMAT, datefmt=_DATEFMT))\n50\t    root.addHandler(handler)\n51\t    root.setLevel(lvl)\n52\t    _configured = True\n53\t    return root\n54\t\n55\t\n56\tdef get_logger(name: str) -> logging.Logger:\n57\t    \"\"\"Convenience wrapper around `logging.getLogger`. Library modules may call this or use\n58\t    `logging.getLogger(__name__)` directly — both inherit the root handler `configure_logging`\n59\t    installs at app startup.\"\"\"\n60\t    return logging.getLogger(name)\n61\t"}]},"uuid":"55f14d99-5e73-4881-898a-e694a71e9965","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:36:06.794Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3ba436dd-5c03-4024-84d8-9a934ac3869b","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Scoped by pubkey so a key\n270\t    rotation starts a fresh lineage.\"\"\"\n271\t    import json as _json\n272\t    store = SpineStore()\n273\t    ok, msg = store.verify()                       # chain: catches a tampered/deleted anchor record\n274\t    if not ok:\n275\t        print(f\"REJECTED: spine integrity failed — {msg}\", file=sys.stderr)\n276\t        sys.exit(2)\n277\t    hok, hmsg = verify_checkpoint(store)           # signed head: catches spine tail-truncation\n278\t    if not hok and \"TAMPER\" in hmsg.upper():\n279\t        print(f\"REJECTED: spine head tampered — {hmsg}\", file=sys.stderr)\n280\t        sys.exit(2)\n281\t    best_count, best_hash = 0, \"\"\n282\t    for r in store.iter_records():\n283\t        if r.kind == \"warden_checkpoint\" and r.payload.get(\"pubkey\") == a.pubkey:\n284\t            c = int(r.payload.get(\"count\", 0))\n285\t            if c >= best_count:\n286\t                best_count, best_hash = c, r.payload.get(\"head_hash\", \"\")\n287\t    print(_json.dumps({\"count\": best_count, \"head_hash\": best_hash}))\n288\t\n289\t\n290\tdef cmd_warden(a) -> None:\n291\t    \"\"\"Phase 6 governor controls (SIGIL §5): kill switch + per-kind promotion policy. owner signing key (the trust anchor)\n300\t    if a.action == \"kill\":\n301\t        seq = KillSwitch(store, owner_key=ok).engage(reason=a.reason or \"\")\n302\t        print(f\"  KILL SWITCH ENGAGED (seq {seq}) — agent mesh halted; perception + memory read stay alive\")\n303\t    elif a.action == \"release\":\n304\t        seq = KillSwitch(store, owner_key=ok).release(reason=a.reason or \"\")\n305\t        print(f\"  kill switch released (seq {seq}, owner-signed) — agent mesh live again\")\n306\t    elif a.action == \"promote\":\n307\t        seq = PromotionPolicy(store, owner_key=ok).grant(a.agent, a.scope or \"*\")\n308\t        print(f\"  refused: {a.agent} has no promotion path (SIGIL §4.6)\" if seq is None\n309\t              else f\"  promoted {a.agent}/{a.scope or '*'} → A2 auto-approve, owner-signed (seq {seq})\")\n310\t    elif a.action == \"revoke\":\n311\t        seq = PromotionPolicy(store, owner_key=ok).revoke(a.agent, a.scope or \"*\")\n312\t        print(f\"  revoked promotion for {a.agent}/{a.scope or '*'} (seq {seq})\")\n313\t\n314\t\n315\tdef cmd_audit(a) -> None:\n316\t    from .audit import render_audit, self_audit\n317\t    store = SpineStore()\n318\t    rows = self_audit(store, agent=a.agent)\n319\t    print(render_audit(rows, agent=a.agent))\n320\t\n321\t\n322\tdef cmd_approve(a) -> None:\n323\t    from .agents.approvals import ApprovalQueue\n324\t    from .governor.identity import ensure_owner_keypair\n325\t    owner_key = ensure_owner_keypair()   # the persisted owner key IS the trusted signer\n326\t    q = ApprovalQueue(SpineStore(), owner_key=owner_key)\n327\t    try:\n328\t        fn = q.approve if a.decision == \"approve\" else q.deny\n329\t        seq = fn(a.seq, approver=a.approver or \"owner\", reason=a.reason or \"\")\n330\t        print(f\"  {a.decision}d queued seq {a.seq} → recorded at seq {seq}  (Ed25519 owner-signed)\")\n331\t    except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001\n332\t        print(f\"  cannot {a.decision} seq {a.seq}: {e}\")\n333\t\n334\t\n335\tdef cmd_dashboard(a) -> None:\n336\t    from .dashboard import render_dashboard, snapshot\n337\t    print(render_dashboard(snapshot(SpineStore())))\n338\t\n339\t\n340\tdef cmd_scrape(a) -> None:\n341\t    \"\"\"SCRIBE grounded web research — crawl PUBLIC, scope-gated, robots-respecting, serve-the-quote.\"\"\"\n342\t    from .scrape import ScrapeScope\n343\t    from .scrape.researcher import WebResearcher\n344\t    from .spine.store import SpineStore\n345\t    if not a.scope_domain:\n346\t        print(\"  --scope-domain is required (deny-all by default): e.g. --scope-domain example.com\")\n347\t        return\n348\t    scope = ScrapeScope(a.scope_domain, include_subdomains=a.subdomains)\n349\t    store = SpineStore()\n350\t    res = WebResearcher(store).research_web(a.question or \"\", a.seed or [], scope)\n351\t    print(f\"  {'; '.join(res.notes)}\")\n352\t    for seq in res.applied:\n353\t        rec = store.get(seq)\n354\t        if rec and rec.payload.get(\"signal\") == \"web.research\":\n355\t            print(\"\\n\" + rec.payload.get(\"text\", \"\"))\n356\t\n357\t\n358\tdef cmd_host(a) -> None:\n359\t    \"\"\"Show this host's capability descriptor (WS-D) — what the mesh routes on.\"\"\"\n360\t    from .platform import host\n361\t    d = host().capabilities()\n362\t    print(f\"  host {d.host_id} · {d.os}\")\n363\t    print(f\"    screen={d.has_screen} camera={d.has_camera} gpu_vlm={d.has_gpu_vlm} always_on={d.always_on}\")\n364\t\n365\t\n366\tdef _device_fingerprint(pubkey: str) -> str:\n367\t    \"\"\"A short, human-verifiable fingerprint of a device pubkey — first 8 bytes of its SHA-256 as\n368\t    `xxxx-xxxx-xxxx-xxxx`. The PC and the future phone client MUST compute this identically so the\n369\t    operator can eyeball-match the code the phone shows before authorizing (defeats a key swap).\"\"\"\n370\t    short = sha256_hex(pubkey.encode())[:16]\n371\t    return \"-\".join(short[i:i + 4] for i in range(0, 16, 4))\n372\t\n373\t\n374\tdef _mesh_authorize(store, device_id, pubkey, owner_key, *, assume_yes=False, confirm=input):\n375\t    \"\"\"Authorize a phone device key (owner-signed). Prints the fingerprint and, unless `assume_yes`,\n376\t    makes the operator confirm it matches what the phone displays before writing the ledger record.\n377\t    Returns the record seq, or None if the operator did not confirm.\"\"\"\n378\t    fp = _device_fingerprint(pubkey)\n379\t    print(f\"  device {device_id!r} fingerprint: {fp}\")\n380\t    if not assume_yes:\n381\t        ans = confirm(f\"  confirm this fingerprint matches what phone {device_id!r} shows? [yes/no] \")\n382\t        if (ans or \"\").strip().lower() != \"yes\":\n383\t            print(\"  aborted — fingerprint not confirmed; device NOT authorized\")\n384\t            return None\n385\t    seq = authorize_device(store, device_id, pubkey, owner_key)\n386\t    print(f\"  authorized device {device_id!r} (owner-signed, seq {seq}); fingerprint {fp}\")\n387\t    return seq\n388\t\n389\t\n390\tdef _mesh_revoke(store, device_id, pubkey, owner_key):\n391\t    \"\"\"Revoke a phone device key (owner-signed). Returns the record seq.\"\"\"\n392\t    seq = revoke_device(store, device_id, pubkey, owner_key)\n393\t    print(f\"  revoked device {device_id!r} (owner-signed, seq {seq}); fingerprint {_device_fingerprint(pubkey)}\")\n394\t    return seq\n395\t\n396\t\n397\tdef _mesh_list(store, owner_pubkey):\n398\t    \"\"\"Print the currently-authorized device keys (owner-signed authorize minus later revoke).\n399\t    Returns the set for callers/tests.\"\"\"\n400\t    devices = authorized_devices(store, owner_pubkey)\n401\t    if not devices:\n402\t        print(\"  no authorized devices\")\n403\t    else:\n404\t        print(f\"  {len(devices)} authorized device key(s):\")\n405\t        for pub in sorted(devices):\n406\t            print(f\"    {pub}  [{_device_fingerprint(pub)}]\")\n407\t    return devices\n408\t\n409\t\n410\tdef cmd_mesh(a) -> None:\n411\t    \"\"\"Phase 9 W1-E — the PC-side mesh pairing back-end: authorize / revoke / list phone device keys.\n412\t    Authorizations are owner-signed via the PERSISTED owner identity (never a supplied key); a thin\n413\t    wrapper over the tested mesh ledger. `list-devices` needs no signing (a fail-closed read).\"\"\"\n414\t    from .governor.identity import ensure_owner_keypair, owner_pubkey\n415\t    store = SpineStore()\n416\t    if a.action == \"list-devices\":\n417\t        _mesh_list(store, owner_pubkey())\n418\t        return\n419\t    if not a.device_id or not a.pubkey:\n420\t        print(f\"  usage: sigil mesh {a.action} <device-id> <pubkey>\", file=sys.stderr)\n421\t        sys.exit(2)\n422\t    owner = ensure_owner_keypair()   # the persisted owner key IS the trusted signer (doctrine)\n423\t    if a.action == \"authorize\":\n424\t        _mesh_authorize(store, a.device_id, a.pubkey, owner, assume_yes=a.yes)\n425\t    elif a.action == \"revoke\":\n426\t        _mesh_revoke(store, a.device_id, a.pubkey, owner)\n427\t\n428\t\n429\tdef cmd_serve(a) -> None:\n430\t    \"\"\"Start the loopback glass-cockpit UI. {"parentUuid":"b0a82f78-7013-4e82-a60e-315d523e437a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"6d6f6da1-0dda-49ba-98c2-ecb094db874a","agentId":"a08348e19783bf157","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014Kv2tSwG8tuBB38DfftjTR","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"The SIGIL bridge server (Phase 9 W1-B) — the WireGuard-bound HTTP transport that lets an\n2\tAUTHORIZED phone reach the desktop `BridgeDaemon`. It forks the WS-C glass-cockpit server\n3\t(`sigil.ui.server`) verbatim in shape (ThreadingHTTPServer + BaseHTTPRequestHandler, the strict\n4\tCSP/nosniff/no-referrer header set, the body cap + 30s timeout, the never-log-auth `log_message`,\n5\tthe SSE structure, the anti-DNS-rebinding Host/Origin/Referer gate) and changes ONLY three things:\n6\t\n7\t  • THE BIND. It binds a `bind_ok` address (loopback or a PRIVATE/WireGuard address) — NEVER\n8\t    0.0.0.0 / a public address. The constructor asserts this; the CLI asserts it too. This is the\n9\t    non-negotiable exposure guard: the tunnel, not the transport, is the network boundary.\n10\t\n11\t  • THE AUTH. There is NO wire bearer secret (the ui's printed token is gone). Authentication IS a\n12\t    per-request Ed25519 signature the phone makes with ITS OWN owner-authorized device key — the\n13\t    Wave-1 device envelope (`bridge.envelope`). The server verifies the signature against the\n14\t    owner-minted authorized-device set (recomputed PER REQUEST so a revocation takes effect at once),\n15\t    binds the authenticated envelope `action` to the endpoint it hit (a `read:pending` envelope can\n16\t    never reach the more-sensitive `read:recall`), applies a wallclock timestamp-freshness window\n17\t    (injectable clock), and for EFFECTFUL actions (panic/relay) additionally runs the envelope's\n18\t    strict monotonic-nonce replay gate (`consume(effectful=True)`). The owner trust-root is NEVER used\n19\t    to sign anything here — the phone signs, the server only verifies.\n20\t\n21\t  • THE ALLOWLIST. The anti-rebind Host/Origin allowlist is derived from the REAL bound address (plus\n22\t    the loopback pair when bound to loopback, for dev), not a hardcoded 127.0.0.1.\n23\t\n24\tEverything sensitive stays off the minimal frames: `/api/pending` and the SSE stream carry only\n25\t`{seq, tier, kind}` — never a subject, never a payload, never a secret. `serve()` now wraps the\n26\ttransport in an owner-PINNED self-signed TLS cert (the phone PWA needs a secure context — https or\n27\tlocalhost — to install and register a service worker; over a bare WireGuard IP that means self-signed\n28\tTLS whose sha256 fingerprint the owner pins once, stable across restarts). `build_server` itself\n29\tstays plain HTTP so the in-tunnel test harness is unchanged and TLS can be terminated upstream if\n30\tpreferred. Offense-free.\"\"\"\n31\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n32\t\n33\timport base64\n34\timport ipaddress\n35\timport json\n36\timport logging\n37\timport os\n38\timport time\n39\tfrom http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer\n40\tfrom pathlib import Path\n41\tfrom urllib.parse import parse_qs, urlparse\n42\t\n43\tfrom ..config import SPINE_PATH\n44\tfrom ..governor.identity import owner_pubkey\n45\tfrom ..mesh import authorized_devices\n46\tfrom ..spine.store import SpineStore\n47\tfrom ..spine.verify import verify_record\n48\tfrom .daemon import BridgeDaemon, bind_ok\n49\tfrom .envelope import consume, verify_envelope\n50\tfrom .notifier import PushNotifier\n51\t\n52\t_WEBAPP = Path(__file__).parent / \"webapp\"       # the PWA is a later slice — served if present, else 404\n53\t_CSP = \"default-src 'self'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'\"\n54\t_DEFAULT_PORT = 8722\n55\t\n56\t# Operational audit trail for a NETWORK-FACING daemon: accepted effectful actions (INFO) and denials\n57\t# (WARNING) go here so an operator can answer \"who did what\" — WITHOUT ever leaking a credential. We\n58\t# log a device pubkey PREFIX only (never the full key), and NEVER the envelope / signature / token /\n59\t# secret / request body. The stdlib access log (`log_message`) stays suppressed for exactly that\n60\t# reason (an envelope can ride in `?env=`); this logger is the operable substitute. Emits only if the\n61\t# host app configures logging (INFO stays silent by default; WARNING surfaces via lastResort).\n62\t_log = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n63\t_PUBKEY_LOG_PREFIX = 12                           # chars of a device pubkey we log — enough to correlate, not the key\n64\t\n65\t\n66\tdef _pk_prefix(pubkey) -> str:\n67\t    \"\"\"A short, non-reversible tag for a device pubkey (prefix only — NEVER the full key in a log).\"\"\"\n68\t    s = str(pubkey) if pubkey else \"\"\n69\t    return (s[:_PUBKEY_LOG_PREFIX] + \"…\") if s else \"?\"\n70\t_TS_WINDOW = 120.0                               # ± seconds a request timestamp may differ from the server clock\n71\t\n72\t# Each network endpoint declares the ONE envelope action that authorizes it. Binding the\n73\t# authenticated action to the endpoint stops a captured lesser-scoped envelope (e.g. `read:pending`,\n74\t# which leaks only {seq,tier,kind}) from being replayed against a more-sensitive endpoint (e.g.\n75\t# `read:recall`, which surfaces the owner's on-screen OCR history). `/api/graph` piggybacks on the\n76\t# `read:snapshot` scope: graph returns only aggregate health, strictly LESS sensitive than the\n77\t# snapshot, so a snapshot-scoped envelope reaching it is not an escalation (and a lesser envelope\n78\t# still cannot reach it).\n79\t_READ_ACTION = {\n80\t    \"/api/pending\": \"read:pending\",\n81\t    \"/api/snapshot\": \"read:snapshot\",\n82\t    \"/api/graph\": \"read:snapshot\",\n83\t    \"/api/stream\": \"read:stream\",\n84\t    \"/api/recall\": \"read:recall\",\n85\t}\n86\t\n87\t\n88\tclass BridgeServer(ThreadingHTTPServer):\n89\t    daemon_threads = True\n90\t\n91\t    def __init__(self, addr, handler, *, spine_path, trusted_pubkey=None, clock=None,\n92\t                 ts_window: float = _TS_WINDOW):\n93\t        host = addr[0]\n94\t        if not bind_ok(host):\n95\t            raise ValueError(\n96\t                f\"refusing to bind {host!r}: the bridge binds loopback or a PRIVATE (WireGuard) \"\n97\t                f\"address only — never 0.0.0.0 / an unspecified / a public address\")\n98\t        super().__init__(addr, handler)\n99\t        self.spine_path = Path(spine_path)\n100\t        # the owner PUBLIC key is the trust anchor (injectable for tests); NO private key ever lives here\n101\t        self.trusted_pubkey = trusted_pubkey if trusted_pubkey is not None else owner_pubkey()\n102\t        self.clock = clock or time.time          # injectable wallclock so the freshness gate is deterministic\n103\t        self.ts_window = ts_window\n104\t        port = self.server_address[1]            # the ACTUAL bound port (correct even for port 0)\n105\t        hosts = {f\"{host}:{port}\"}\n106\t        origins = {f\"http://{host}:{port}\"}\n107\t        if ipaddress.ip_address(host).is_loopback:   # dev convenience only — not added for a WG bind\n108\t            hosts |= {f\"127.0.0.1:{port}\", f\"localhost:{port}\"}\n109\t            origins |= {f\"http://127.0.0.1:{port}\", f\"http://localhost:{port}\"}\n110\t        self.allowed_hosts = frozenset(hosts)\n111\t        self.allowed_origins = frozenset(origins)\n112\t\n113\t    def store(self) -> SpineStore:\n114\t        return SpineStore(self.spine_path)       # fresh read each request (cheap, current)\n115\t\n116\t    def daemon(self) -> BridgeDaemon:\n117\t        return BridgeDaemon(self.store(), trusted_pubkey=self.trusted_pubkey)\n118\t\n119\t\n120\tdef _json_bytes(obj) -> bytes:\n121\t    return json.dumps(obj, ensure_ascii=False).encode(\"utf-8\")\n122\t\n123\t\n124\tclass Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):\n125\t    server_version = \"sigil-bridge/1.0\"\n126\t    timeout = 30                                  # per-connection socket timeout (no hung reader)\n127\t    _MAX_BODY = 65536                             # bodies are tiny; cap to avoid a Content-Length hang/alloc\n128\t\n129\t    # never log auth (an envelope can ride in ?env= for SSE; never write it to a log)\n130\t    def log_message(self, fmt, *args):  # noqa: A003\n131\t        pass\n132\t\n133\t    # --- request helpers --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n134\t    def _query(self) -> dict:\n135\t        return parse_qs(urlparse(self.path).query)\n136\t\n137\t    def _authorized_now(self):\n138\t        \"\"\"The owner-minted authorized-device set, recomputed PER REQUEST — a revoke takes effect now.\"\"\"\n139\t        return authorized_devices(self.server.store(), self.server.trusted_pubkey)\n140\t\n141\t    def _envelope_payload(self):\n142\t        \"\"\"The device envelope: base64url of canonical JSON, in `X-SIGIL-Envelope` (GET/POST) or the\n143\t        `?env=` query (SSE, which cannot set a header). Returns the payload dict, or None if absent/\n144\t        malformed (fail-closed — the caller denies).\"\"\"\n145\t        raw = self.headers.get(\"X-SIGIL-Envelope\") or (self._query().get(\"env\", [\"\"])[0])\n146\t        if not raw:\n147\t            return None\n148\t        try:\n149\t            data = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(raw + \"=\" * (-len(raw) % 4))\n150\t            payload = json.loads(data)\n151\t        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — any decode/parse failure is an unauthenticated request\n152\t            return None\n153\t        return payload if isinstance(payload, dict) else None\n154\t\n155\t    def _fresh(self, core) -> bool:\n156\t        \"\"\"The server-layer wallclock freshness window (the envelope module is pure — it never reads a\n157\t        clock — so freshness lives HERE). A non-numeric / far-off timestamp fails closed.\"\"\"\n158\t        try:\n159\t            ts = float(core.get(\"ts\"))\n160\t        except (TypeError, ValueError):\n161\t            return False\n162\t        return abs(self.server.clock() - ts) <= self.server.ts_window\n163\t\n164\t    def _authed(self, action: str):\n165\t        \"\"\"Authenticate a READ: verify the device signature, apply freshness, bind the action to this\n166\t        endpoint. Reads are side-effect-free, so they are NOT receipted (no spine write per GET) — the\n167\t        freshness window is their replay bound. Returns the core, or None after sending a deny.\"\"\"\n168\t        payload = self._envelope_payload()\n169\t        if payload is None:\n170\t            self._deny(401, \"missing/invalid device envelope\")\n171\t            return None\n172\t        ok, core = verify_envelope(payload, self._authorized_now())\n173\t        if not ok:\n174\t            self._deny(401, f\"unauthenticated: {core}\")\n175\t            return None\n176\t        if not self._fresh(core):\n177\t            self._deny(401, \"stale request (timestamp outside freshness window)\")\n178\t            return None\n179\t        if core.get(\"action\") != action:\n180\t            self._deny(403, \"envelope action does not authorize this endpoint\")\n181\t            return None\n182\t        return core\n183\t\n184\t    def _authed_effectful(self, action: str):\n185\t        \"\"\"Authenticate an EFFECTFUL request (panic/relay): verify + freshness + endpoint-bind, then run\n186\t        the envelope's strict monotonic-nonce replay gate and receipt it on the spine\n187\t        (`consume(effectful=True)`). A replayed/stale-nonce envelope is refused. Returns core or None.\"\"\"\n188\t        payload = self._envelope_payload()\n189\t        if payload is None:\n190\t            self._deny(401, \"missing/invalid device envelope\")\n191\t            return None\n192\t        authorized = self._authorized_now()\n193\t        ok, core = verify_envelope(payload, authorized)\n194\t        if not ok:\n195\t            self._deny(401, f\"unauthenticated: {core}\")\n196\t            return None\n197\t        if not self._fresh(core):\n198\t            self._deny(401, \"stale request (timestamp outside freshness window)\")\n199\t            return None\n200\t        if core.get(\"action\") != action:\n201\t            self._deny(403, \"envelope action does not authorize this endpoint\")\n202\t            return None\n203\t        try:\n204\t            return consume(self.server.store(), payload, authorized, effectful=True)\n205\t        except ValueError as e:                   # replay / invalid nonce (fail-closed)\n206\t            self._deny(409, f\"refused: {str(e)[:120]}\")\n207\t            return None\n208\t        except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 — never let a consume error crash the handler (no RemoteDisconnected)\n209\t            self._deny(400, f\"refused: {type(e).__name__}\")\n210\t            return None\n211\t\n212\t    def _rebind_ok(self) -> bool:\n213\t        \"\"\"The anti-DNS-rebinding gate (ui `_action_ok` logic, minus the token — the envelope is the\n214\t        credential): the `Host` must be in the WG-derived allowlist, an `Origin`, if present, must\n215\t        EXACT-match an allowed origin (a prefix like `http://IP:PORT.evil.com` must NOT pass), and a\n216\t        `Referer`, if present, must sit under an allowed origin. Applied to the whole POST action\n217\t        plane as defense-in-depth.\"\"\"\n218\t        if self.headers.get(\"Host\", \"\") not in self.server.allowed_hosts:\n219\t            return False\n220\t        o = self.headers.get(\"Origin\") or \"\"\n221\t        ref = self.headers.get(\"Referer\") or \"\"\n222\t        if o and o not in self.server.allowed_origins:\n223\t            return False\n224\t        if ref and not any(ref.startswith(a + \"/\") or ref == a for a in self.server.allowed_origins):\n225\t            return False\n226\t        return True\n227\t\n228\t    # --- response helpers -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n229\t    def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype=\"application/json\"):\n230\t        self.send_response(code)\n231\t        self.send_header(\"Content-Type\", ctype)\n232\t        self.send_header(\"Content-Length\", str(len(body)))\n233\t        self.send_header(\"Content-Security-Policy\", _CSP)\n234\t        self.send_header(\"X-Content-Type-Options\", \"nosniff\")\n235\t        self.send_header(\"Referrer-Policy\", \"no-referrer\")\n236\t        self.end_headers()\n237\t        try:\n238\t            self.wfile.write(body)\n239\t        except (BrokenPipeError, ConnectionResetError):\n240\t            pass\n241\t\n242\t    def _json(self, obj, code=200):\n243\t        self._send(code, _json_bytes(obj))\n244\t\n245\t    def _deny(self, code=403, msg=\"forbidden\"):\n246\t        # WARNING-level audit of every denial: method, PATH ONLY (never the query — an envelope can ride\n247\t        # in `?env=`), status, and the developer-controlled reason. `msg` is a fixed reason string, never\n248\t        # the envelope/signature/token/secret/body.\n249\t        _log.warning(\"bridge denied: %s %s -> %d (%s)\",\n250\t                     getattr(self, \"command\", \"?\"), urlparse(self.path).path, code, msg)\n251\t        self._json({\"error\": msg}, code)\n252\t\n253\t    # --- GET (read plane) -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n254\t    def do_GET(self):\n255\t        path = urlparse(self.path).path\n256\t        if path in (\"/\", \"/index.html\"):\n257\t            return self._serve_index()\n258\t        if path.startswith(\"/static/\"):\n259\t            return self._serve_static(path[len(\"/static/\"):])\n260\t        if not path.startswith(\"/api/\"):\n261\t            return self._deny(404, \"not found\")\n262\t        if path == \"/api/stream\":\n263\t            return self._sse()                    # SSE authenticates via ?env= inside _authed\n264\t        if path == \"/api/pending\":\n265\t            if self._authed(_READ_ACTION[path]) is None:\n266\t                return\n267\t            return self._json({\"pending\": self.server.daemon().pending()})   # {seq,tier,kind} only\n268\t        if path == \"/api/snapshot\":\n269\t            if self._authed(_READ_ACTION[path]) is None:\n270\t                return\n271\t            from ..dashboard import snapshot\n272\t            return self._json(snapshot(self.server.store()))\n273\t        if path == \"/api/graph\":\n274\t            if self._authed(_READ_ACTION[path]) is None:\n275\t                return\n276\t            return self._graph()\n277\t        if path.startswith(\"/api/record/\"):\n278\t            if self._authed(\"read:record\") is None:\n279\t                return\n280\t            return self._record(path.rsplit(\"/\", 1)[-1])\n281\t        if path == \"/api/recall\":\n282\t            if self._authed(_READ_ACTION[path]) is None:\n283\t                return\n284\t            subject = self._query().get(\"subject\", [\"\"])[0]\n285\t            return self._json({\"subject\": subject, \"recall\": self.server.daemon().recall(subject)})\n286\t        return self._deny(404, \"unknown endpoint\")\n287\t\n288\t    def _serve_index(self):\n289\t        idx = _WEBAPP / \"index.html\"\n290\t        if not idx.is_file():\n291\t            return self._deny(404, \"bridge PWA not built yet\")   # graceful — the webapp is a later slice\n292\t        try:\n293\t            self._send(200, idx.read_bytes(), ctype=\"text/html; charset=utf-8\")\n294\t        except OSError:\n295\t            self._deny(404, \"not found\")\n296\t\n297\t    def _serve_static(self, sub: str):\n298\t        base = _WEBAPP.resolve()\n299\t        target = (base / sub).resolve()\n300\t        if base != target and base not in target.parents:        # traversal guard (allowlist by containment)\n301\t            return self._deny(404, \"not found\")\n302\t        if not target.is_file():\n303\t            return self._deny(404, \"not found\")                  # includes: webapp dir absent (later slice)\n304\t        name = target.name\n305\t        ctype = (\"application/javascript\" if name.endswith(\".js\")\n306\t                 else \"text/css\" if name.endswith(\".css\")\n307\t                 else \"text/html\" if name.endswith(\".html\")\n308\t                 else \"application/octet-stream\")\n309\t        try:\n310\t            self._send(200, target.read_bytes(), ctype=f\"{ctype}; charset=utf-8\")\n311\t        except OSError:\n312\t            self._deny(404, \"not found\")\n313\t\n314\t    def _record(self, raw):\n315\t        try:\n316\t            seq = int(raw)\n317\t        except ValueError:\n318\t            return self._deny(400, \"bad seq\")\n319\t        rec = self.server.store().get(seq)\n320\t        if rec is None:\n321\t            return self._json({\"error\": \"no such record\", \"seq\": seq,\n322\t                               \"note\": \"no grounded record — not fabricated\"}, 404)\n323\t        ok, reason = verify_record(rec)                          # re-verify the atom LIVE (prove-don't-guess)\n324\t        self._json({\"seq\": rec.seq, \"kind\": rec.kind, \"source\": rec.source, \"actor\": rec.actor,\n325\t                    \"ts\": rec.ts, \"entry_hash\": rec.entry_hash, \"prev_hash\": rec.prev_hash,\n326\t                    \"payload\": rec.payload, \"integrity_ok\": ok, \"integrity_reason\": reason})\n327\t\n328\t    def _graph(self):\n329\t        try:\n330\t            from ..graph import health\n331\t            self._json({\"health\": health()})\n332\t        except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 — graph may not be built yet (ImportError included)\n333\t            self._json({\"error\": \"graph unavailable\", \"note\": str(e)[:200]})\n334\t\n335\t    def _sse(self):\n336\t        if self._authed(\"read:stream\") is None:                  # deny already sent (401/403)\n337\t            return\n338\t        self.send_response(200)\n339\t        self.send_header(\"Content-Type\", \"text/event-stream\")\n340\t        self.send_header(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-cache\")\n341\t        self.send_header(\"Content-Security-Policy\", _CSP)\n342\t        self.send_header(\"X-Content-Type-Options\", \"nosniff\")\n343\t        self.send_header(\"Referrer-Policy\", \"no-referrer\")\n344\t        self.end_headers()\n345\t        try:\n346\t            since = int(self._query().get(\"since\", [\"-1\"])[0])\n347\t        except (ValueError, TypeError):\n348\t            since = -1                                            # any malformed cursor → from genesis\n349\t        notifier = PushNotifier(self.server.store(), since_seq=since)\n350\t        try:\n351\t            while True:\n352\t                sent = False\n353\t                for ev in notifier.poll():\n354\t                    frame = {\"seq\": ev[\"seq\"], \"tier\": ev[\"tier\"], \"kind\": ev[\"kind\"]}   # minimal — no subject\n355\t                    self.wfile.write(f\"data: {json.dumps(frame, ensure_ascii=False)}\\n\\n\".encode(\"utf-8\"))\n356\t                    sent = True\n357\t                self.wfile.write(b\": hb\\n\\n\")                     # heartbeat / flush\n358\t                self.wfile.flush()\n359\t                if not sent:\n360\t                    time.sleep(0.25)\n361\t        except (BrokenPipeError, ConnectionResetError, OSError):\n362\t            return                                                # client closed — end the stream\n363\t\n364\t    # --- POST (action plane) ----------------------------------------------------------------------\n365\t    def do_POST(self):\n366\t        path = urlparse(self.path).path\n367\t        if path not in (\"/api/action\", \"/api/panic\", \"/api/relay\", \"/api/gesture/arm\"):\n368\t            return self._deny(404, \"not found\")\n369\t        try:                                                     # a missing/non-numeric/negative CL is a\n370\t            length = int(self.headers.get(\"Content-Length\", \"0\") or \"0\")   # clean 400 — never an unhandled\n371\t        except (TypeError, ValueError):                          # ValueError that crashes the handler thread\n372\t            return self._deny(400, \"invalid Content-Length header\")\n373\t        if length < 0:\n374\t            return self._deny(400, \"invalid Content-Length header\")\n375\t        if length > self._MAX_BODY:                              # cap the body (no CL hang / alloc)\n376\t            return self._deny(413, \"body too large\")\n377\t        body_bytes = self.rfile.read(length) if length else b\"\"\n378\t        if not self._rebind_ok():                               # anti-rebind gate on the whole action plane\n379\t            return self._deny(403, \"action denied (origin / host — possible DNS rebinding)\")\n380\t        if path == \"/api/action\":\n381\t            return self._device_action(body_bytes)\n382\t        if path == \"/api/gesture/arm\":\n383\t            return self._device_arm(body_bytes)\n384\t        # panic / relay — effectful, envelope-authenticated with the strict nonce replay gate\n385\t        action = \"panic\" if path == \"/api/panic\" else \"relay\"\n386\t        core = self._authed_effectful(action)\n387\t        if core is None:\n388\t            return\n389\t        dev = _pk_prefix(core.get(\"device\"))                    # device pubkey PREFIX only — never the full key\n390\t        if action == \"panic\":\n391\t            seq = self.server.daemon().panic_engage(by=\"phone\")\n392\t            _log.info(\"bridge action ok: POST %s device=%s outcome=panic-engaged seq=%s\", path, dev, seq)\n393\t            return self._json({\"ok\": True, \"seq\": seq})\n394\t        a = core.get(\"args\")                                     # the relayed command is INSIDE the signed core;\n395\t        text = str(a.get(\"text\", \"\")) if isinstance(a, dict) else \"\"   # signed args may be ANY JSON type — guard\n396\t        reply = self.server.daemon().relay(text)                # NB: never log the relayed text (may hold a subject)\n397\t        _log.info(\"bridge action ok: POST %s device=%s outcome=relayed\", path, dev)\n398\t        return self._json({\"ok\": True, \"reply\": reply})\n399\t\n400\t    def _device_action(self, body_bytes):\n401\t        \"\"\"The phone posts its OWN device-signed `governor.approval` payload. The SERVER only VERIFIES\n402\t        (it never signs) — `submit_device_approval` accepts it iff the signing device is currently\n403\t        authorized AND the signature verifies; the signed `target_seq` binds, so no nonce is needed.\"\"\"\n404\t        try:\n405\t            body = json.loads(body_bytes or b\"{}\")\n406\t        except (ValueError, TypeError) as e:\n407\t            return self._deny(400, f\"bad request: {e}\")\n408\t        if not isinstance(body, dict):\n409\t            return self._deny(400, \"bad request: body must be a JSON object\")\n410\t        try:\n411\t            seq = self.server.daemon().submit_device_approval(body)\n412\t        except ValueError as e:                                  # unauthorized / forged / not-an-approval\n413\t            return self._deny(403, f\"approval refused: {str(e)[:200]}\")\n414\t        except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 — never leak internals as a 500\n415\t            return self._deny(400, f\"action failed: {str(e)[:200]}\")\n416\t        _log.info(\"bridge action ok: POST /api/action device=%s outcome=approval-recorded seq=%s\",\n417\t                  _pk_prefix(body.get(\"pubkey\")), seq)          # pubkey PREFIX only — never the sig/body\n418\t        self._json({\"ok\": True, \"seq\": seq})\n419\t\n420\t    def _device_arm(self, body_bytes):\n421\t        \"\"\"The phone posts its OWN device-signed gesture arm request. The SERVER only VERIFIES + RECORDS\n422\t        it (`submit_arm_request`, fail-closed on unauthorized/forged); the live SessionGate re-verifies\n423\t        and enforces freshness / kill-switch / single-session / TTL when it consumes it. Recording is\n424\t        necessary-but-not-sufficient to arm — the trust boundary is `arm_by_device`, not this endpoint.\"\"\"\n425\t        try:\n426\t            body = json.loads(body_bytes or b\"{}\")\n427\t        except (ValueError, TypeError) as e:\n428\t            return self._deny(400, f\"bad request: {e}\")\n429\t        if not isinstance(body, dict):\n430\t            return self._deny(400, \"bad request: body must be a JSON object\")\n431\t        try:\n432\t            seq = self.server.daemon().submit_arm_request(body)\n433\t        except ValueError as e:                                  # unauthorized / forged / not-an-arm-request\n434\t            return self._deny(403, f\"arm refused: {str(e)[:200]}\")\n435\t        except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 — never leak internals as a 500\n436\t            return self._deny(400, f\"arm failed: {str(e)[:200]}\")\n437\t        _log.info(\"bridge action ok: POST /api/gesture/arm device=%s outcome=arm-recorded seq=%s\",\n438\t                  _pk_prefix(body.get(\"pubkey\")), seq)          # pubkey PREFIX only — never the sig/body\n439\t        self._json({\"ok\": True, \"seq\": seq})\n440\t\n441\t\n442\tdef build_server(*, addr: str, port: int = _DEFAULT_PORT, spine_path=None, trusted_pubkey=None,\n443\t                 clock=None) -> BridgeServer:\n444\t    \"\"\"Build (but do not run) the bridge server bound to `addr` (asserted `bind_ok`). `trusted_pubkey`\n445\t    and `clock` are injectable for deterministic tests; both default to the real owner identity / wallclock.\"\"\"\n446\t    return BridgeServer((addr, port), Handler,\n447\t                        spine_path=Path(spine_path) if spine_path else SPINE_PATH,\n448\t                        trusted_pubkey=trusted_pubkey, clock=clock)\n449\t\n450\t\n451\tdef _write_secure(path: Path, data: bytes) -> None:\n452\t    \"\"\"Write `data` to `path` created 0600 up-front (no world-readable window) — the vault/secrets\n453\t    secure-file idiom (`os.open(..., 0o600)`).\"\"\"\n454\t    fd = os.open(str(path), os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_TRUNC, 0o600)\n455\t    with os.fdopen(fd, \"wb\") as fh:\n456\t        fh.write(data)\n457\t\n458\t\n459\tdef _fingerprint(cert) -> str:\n460\t    \"\"\"The pinned identity: sha256 of the DER cert, hex, colon-grouped byte pairs (openssl-style,\n461\t    upper-case) — what the owner eyeball-matches on the phone once.\"\"\"\n462\t    from cryptography.hazmat.primitives import hashes\n463\t    h = cert.fingerprint(hashes.SHA256()).hex().upper()      # fingerprint() == sha256 of the DER encoding\n464\t    return \":\".join(h[i:i + 2] for i in range(0, len(h), 2))\n465\t\n466\t\n467\tdef ensure_bridge_cert(addr: str) -> tuple[str, str, str]:\n468\t    \"\"\"Mint (or REUSE) a minimal owner-pinned self-signed TLS cert for the bridge bound to `addr`,\n469\t    returning `(certfile, keyfile, sha256_fingerprint)`.\n470\t\n471\t    The cert carries `addr` as its CN and (when `addr` is an IP — always true for a `bind_ok` bridge)\n472\t    as an `x509.IPAddress` SubjectAlternativeName, so an IP-literal TLS connection matches. Cert+key\n473\t    live under `SIGIL_HOME/bridge/` (dir 0700, files 0600). If a cert already exists FOR THIS ADDR it\n474\t    is REUSED — the fingerprint is therefore STABLE across restarts, so the owner pins it exactly once\n475\t    (a changed fingerprint later means a MITM / a wiped key, not a benign restart). An EC P-256 key is\n476\t    used (easy for `cryptography`, accepted by `ssl`). Validity ~2 years; a real wallclock is fine here\n477\t    (this is TLS notBefore/notAfter, not spine or learning math).\"\"\"\n478\t    import datetime\n479\t    import ipaddress as _ip\n480\t\n481\t    from cryptography import x509\n482\t    from cryptography.hazmat.primitives import hashes, serialization\n483\t    from cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric import ec\n484\t    from cryptography.x509.oid import NameOID\n485\t\n486\t    from ..config import SIGIL_HOME                          # read at call-time (patchable in tests)\n487\t\n488\t    bridge_dir = Path(SIGIL_HOME) / \"bridge\"\n489\t    bridge_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n490\t    try:\n491\t        os.chmod(bridge_dir, 0o700)                          # owner-only, like the spine/keys dirs\n492\t    except OSError:\n493\t        pass\n494\t    tag = addr.replace(\":\", \"_\").replace(\"/\", \"_\").replace(\"%\", \"_\")   # one stable cert per bind addr\n495\t    certfile = bridge_dir / f\"cert-{tag}.pem\"\n496\t    keyfile = bridge_dir / f\"key-{tag}.pem\"\n497\t\n498\t    if certfile.is_file() and keyfile.is_file():             # REUSE → stable fingerprint (pin once)\n499\t        cert = x509.load_pem_x509_certificate(certfile.read_bytes())\n500\t        return str(certfile), str(keyfile), _fingerprint(cert)\n501\t\n502\t    key = ec.generate_private_key(ec.SECP256R1())\n503\t    name = x509.Name([x509.NameAttribute(NameOID.COMMON_NAME, addr)])\n504\t    try:                                                     # bind addr is an IP by construction (bind_ok)\n505\t        san = x509.SubjectAlternativeName([x509.IPAddress(_ip.ip_address(addr))])\n506\t    except ValueError:                                       # defensive: a hostname would use a DNS SAN\n507\t        san = x509.SubjectAlternativeName([x509.DNSName(addr)])\n508\t    now = datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc)       # real wallclock (tz-aware; TLS validity, not spine math)\n509\t    cert = (\n510\t        x509.CertificateBuilder()\n511\t        .subject_name(name)\n512\t        .issuer_name(name)                                   # self-signed: issuer == subject\n513\t        .public_key(key.public_key())\n514\t        .serial_number(x509.random_serial_number())\n515\t        .not_valid_before(now - datetime.timedelta(minutes=5))   # small skew tolerance\n516\t        .not_valid_after(now + datetime.timedelta(days=730))     # ~2 years\n517\t        .add_extension(san, critical=False)\n518\t        .add_extension(x509.BasicConstraints(ca=False, path_length=None), critical=True)\n519\t        .sign(key, hashes.SHA256())\n520\t    )\n521\t    _write_secure(certfile, cert.public_bytes(serialization.Encoding.PEM))\n522\t    _write_secure(keyfile, key.private_bytes(serialization.Encoding.PEM,\n523\t                                             serialization.PrivateFormat.PKCS8,\n524\t                                             serialization.NoEncryption()))\n525\t    return str(certfile), str(keyfile), _fingerprint(cert)\n526\t\n527\t\n528\tdef serve(*, addr: str, port: int = _DEFAULT_PORT, spine_path=None, tls: bool = True) -> None:\n529\t    \"\"\"Run the WireGuard-bound bridge transport. With `tls=True` (default) it wraps the listening\n530\t    socket in an owner-pinned self-signed TLS cert (`ensure_bridge_cert`) BEFORE serving, printing the\n531\t    `https://` bind URL + the sha256 fingerprint to pin on the phone. With `tls=False` it serves plain\n532\t    HTTP (degraded: no secure context → the PWA cannot install / register a service worker) and prints\n533\t    an honest warning. `build_server` stays plain — TLS lives only here.\"\"\"\n534\t    srv = build_server(addr=addr, port=port, spine_path=spine_path)\n535\t    bound = srv.server_address[1]\n536\t    if tls:\n537\t        import ssl\n538\t        certfile, keyfile, fp = ensure_bridge_cert(addr)\n539\t        ctx = ssl.SSLContext(ssl.PROTOCOL_TLS_SERVER)\n540\t        ctx.load_cert_chain(certfile, keyfile)\n541\t        srv.socket = ctx.wrap_socket(srv.socket, server_side=True)   # wrap the listener before serve_forever\n542\t        print(f\"  SIGIL bridge → https://{addr}:{bound}/   (WireGuard-bound; loopback/private only — NO wire secret)\")\n543\t        print(f\"  TLS fingerprint (sha256): {fp}\")\n544\t        print(\"    ↳ PIN this once on the phone — it is stable across restarts; a later change means MITM\")\n545\t    else:\n546\t        print(f\"  SIGIL bridge → http://{addr}:{bound}/   (WireGuard-bound; loopback/private only — NO wire secret)\")\n547\t        print(\"  WARNING: --no-tls → plain HTTP, NO secure context: the phone CANNOT install the PWA or\")\n548\t        print(\"           register a service worker. Use TLS unless you terminate it upstream in the tunnel.\")\n549\t    print(\"  pair a phone (owner, at the desktop):  sigil mesh authorize <device-id> <device-pubkey>\")\n550\t    print(\"  every request must carry an authorized-device signature (X-SIGIL-Envelope) — there is no token\")\n551\t    _serve_until_signal(srv)\n552\t\n553\t\n554\tdef _serve_until_signal(srv) -> None:\n555\t    \"\"\"Serve until SIGTERM/SIGINT, then shut down CLEANLY so `systemd stop` is graceful, not an abrupt\n556\t    kill. `serve_forever()` runs on a worker thread; a signal (delivered to the MAIN thread) sets a\n557\t    stop event, and shutdown is driven from THIS (main) thread — a different thread from the one inside\n558\t    `serve_forever`, which is required (calling `shutdown()` from within its own thread deadlocks). We\n559\t    then `server_close()` to release the (possibly TLS-wrapped) listening socket.\"\"\"\n560\t    import signal\n561\t    import threading\n562\t\n563\t    stop = threading.Event()\n564\t\n565\t    def _on_signal(signum, _frame):\n566\t        _log.info(\"bridge: received signal %d — shutting down gracefully\", signum)\n567\t        stop.set()\n568\t\n569\t    for _sig in (signal.SIGTERM, signal.SIGINT):\n570\t        try:\n571\t            signal.signal(_sig, _on_signal)\n572\t        except (ValueError, OSError):        # not the main thread / platform-unsupported — degrade below\n573\t            pass\n574\t\n575\t    worker = threading.Thread(target=srv.serve_forever, name=\"sigil-bridge-serve\", daemon=True)\n576\t    worker.start()\n577\t    try:\n578\t        while not stop.wait(1.0):            # poll the flag so a delivered signal is always noticed\n579\t            pass\n580\t    except KeyboardInterrupt:               # belt-and-suspenders if SIGINT could not be installed above\n581\t        stop.set()\n582\t    finally:\n583\t        srv.shutdown()                      # stop serve_forever (from a DIFFERENT thread — no deadlock)\n584\t        srv.server_close()                  # close the (TLS-wrapped) listening socket\n585\t        worker.join(timeout=5)\n586\t        _log.info(\"bridge: stopped — listening socket closed\")\n587\t"}]},"uuid":"919fd8dc-1c40-4dff-bbea-3a6659e5acce","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:36:34.001Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b0a82f78-7013-4e82-a60e-315d523e437a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"919fd8dc-1c40-4dff-bbea-3a6659e5acce","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a08348e19783bf157","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdBGYJr8Eg9f61q58TmEJ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01569nowrVPBYRn5dU66XgHq","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd /home/kali/sigil && echo \"===== bridge/daemon.py (bind_ok) =====\"; sed -n '1,80p' sigil/bridge/daemon.py 2>/dev/null; echo; echo \"===== pyproject console_scripts / entry points =====\"; grep -n -A6 \"console_scripts\\|project.scripts\\|\\[project.scripts\\]\\|scripts\" pyproject.toml","description":"Read bridge daemon bind_ok and pyproject scripts"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":14817,"cache_read_input_tokens":43352,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":14817,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":6,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdBGYGpakWd55WsGuRRvP","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"9b419b1b-2d5f-4e70-9cc6-70fdb447d268","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:36:34.614Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"9b419b1b-2d5f-4e70-9cc6-70fdb447d268","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"6d6f6da1-0dda-49ba-98c2-ecb094db874a","agentId":"a08348e19783bf157","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01569nowrVPBYRn5dU66XgHq","type":"tool_result","content":"===== bridge/daemon.py (bind_ok) =====\n\"\"\"BridgeDaemon (Phase 7, WS-D D-v) — the desktop-side API the phone talks to over WireGuard. It\nreuses the owner-signed cores: the queue (`approvals.pending` with the authorized-device set), a\nDEVICE-signed approval (accepted only while that device is authorized), a PANIC halt (any engage is\nfail-safe), and a KERNEL command relay (`KernelDispatch` → the same WARDEN gate as voice/UI).\n\nThis module is the bridge LIBRARY CORE + API (the doctrine + crypto). The network TRANSPORT — an\nHTTP server over WireGuard, reusing the WS-C two-plane server pattern bound to a `bind_ok` address —\nis a documented NEXT slice, not shipped here. `bind_ok` is the bind predicate that transport MUST\nuse: loopback or a PRIVATE (WireGuard) address only — NEVER 0.0.0.0 / a public address.\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport ipaddress\nimport time\nfrom typing import List, Optional\n\nfrom ..spine.store import SpineStore\n\n\n_CGNAT4 = ipaddress.ip_network(\"100.64.0.0/10\")   # carrier-grade NAT — the range Tailscale assigns tailnet IPs\n_DEDUP_WINDOW = 2048                               # bound the replay-dedup scan to the most recent N records\n\n\ndef bind_ok(addr: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"True iff `addr` is safe to bind: loopback, a PRIVATE (e.g. WireGuard) address, or the CGNAT range\n    Tailscale uses — never 0.0.0.0 / a public / an unspecified address.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        ip = ipaddress.ip_address(addr)\n    except ValueError:\n        return False\n    if ip.is_unspecified:                          # 0.0.0.0 / :: → refuse\n        return False\n    return ip.is_loopback or ip.is_private or (ip.version == 4 and ip in _CGNAT4)\n\n\nclass BridgeDaemon:\n    def __init__(self, store: Optional[SpineStore] = None, *, trusted_pubkey: Optional[str] = None):\n        from ..governor.identity import owner_pubkey\n        self.store = store or SpineStore()\n        # the owner trust anchor — the persisted identity by default (injectable for tests)\n        self.trusted_pubkey = trusted_pubkey if trusted_pubkey is not None else owner_pubkey()\n\n    def _authorized(self):\n        from ..mesh import authorized_devices\n        return authorized_devices(self.store, self.trusted_pubkey)\n\n    def pending(self) -> List[dict]:\n        \"\"\"The phone-facing queue — minimal fields only (no subject leaks over the tunnel).\"\"\"\n        from ..agents.approvals import pending\n        items = pending(self.store, self.trusted_pubkey, extra_pubkeys=self._authorized())\n        return [{\"seq\": r.seq, \"tier\": r.payload.get(\"tier\"), \"kind\": r.kind} for r in items]\n\n    def recall(self, subject: str) -> Optional[dict]:\n        \"\"\"Read-only remote RECALL — \"where did I last see X?\" — answered from the owner's own\n        GROUNDED on-screen OCR history. Returns the perception-recall provenance dict VERBATIM\n        (seq/entry_hash/when/frame_sha256/quote) or None: A0, read-only, no VLM and no paraphrase —\n        the served `quote` is the owner's verbatim captured OCR line, never an advisory VLM lead.\n\n        This surfaces the owner's own on-screen text over the tunnel, so it leaks MORE than\n        `pending()`'s minimal {seq,tier,kind} fields. The network SERVER (a later slice) MUST\n        therefore gate this behind an authorized-device (device-signed) read request; this daemon\n        method is only the read-only core.\"\"\"\n        from ..perception.recall import recall\n        return recall(self.store, subject)\n\n    def submit_device_approval(self, payload: dict) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Append a DEVICE-signed approval the phone produced — ONLY if the signing device is\n        currently authorized AND the signature verifies (fail-closed). A rogue/revoked device or a\n        forged signature is refused; the signed target_seq binds (no replay).\"\"\"\n        from ..agents.approvals import SIGNAL as APPROVAL_SIGNAL\n        from ..agents.approvals import verify_approval\n\n        class _Rec:\n            def __init__(self, p): self.payload = p\n        authorized = self._authorized()\n        if payload.get(\"signal\") != APPROVAL_SIGNAL:\n            raise ValueError(\"not an approval record\")\n        if payload.get(\"pubkey\") not in authorized:\n            raise ValueError(\"signing device is not authorized\")\n        if not verify_approval(_Rec(payload), self.trusted_pubkey, extra_pubkeys=authorized):\n            raise ValueError(\"approval signature invalid\")\n\n===== pyproject console_scripts / entry points =====\n53:[project.scripts]\n54-sigil = \"sigil.cli:main\"\n55-\n56-[tool.setuptools.dynamic]\n57-version = { attr = \"sigil.__version__\" }\n58-\n59-[tool.setuptools.packages.find]","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"8221115e-6afe-45ef-8808-b647bb405afc","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:36:37.471Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"9b419b1b-2d5f-4e70-9cc6-70fdb447d268","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"d4e98156-49e9-422e-ada4-9aa6aa6fc594","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a08348e19783bf157","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdBGa9KCK3Q8gqdknJhR6","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_011ooTgf1vydxa8ZjPuMK7XA","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd /home/kali/sigil && echo \"===== MCP server head =====\"; sed -n '1,60p' sigil/mcp/server.py; echo; echo \"===== README: install / mcp / qdrant / kernel mentions =====\"; grep -n -iE \"pip install|install -e|\\.\\[all\\]|claude mcp|mcp add|qdrant|docker|build.*kernel|cargo build|sigil doctor|register|console|mcp server|SIGIL_HOME\" README.md | head -60","description":"Read MCP server head and grep README for install steps"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":17022,"cache_read_input_tokens":58169,"output_tokens":717,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":17022},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":1,"output_tokens":717,"cache_read_input_tokens":58169,"cache_creation_input_tokens":17022,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":17022,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdBGa7z5UyT7qKd9QALvp","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"ded13c2a-4b3d-46a9-8345-1ed736d99b55","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:36:49.770Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"d4e98156-49e9-422e-ada4-9aa6aa6fc594","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"6d6f6da1-0dda-49ba-98c2-ecb094db874a","agentId":"a08348e19783bf157","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SDJgbNdm5SpTEG9oGMaEfq","type":"tool_result","content":"===== pyproject.toml (full) =====\n# SIGIL — a local-first, offense-free personal AI orchestrator.\n# PEP 621 metadata. Classic setuptools backend (do NOT require bleeding-edge setuptools — stock LTS ships\n# an older version, and PEP 639 SPDX license expressions need >=77).\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"setuptools>=61\", \"wheel\"]\nbuild-backend = \"setuptools.build_meta\"\n\n[project]\nname = \"sigil\"\ndescription = \"A local-first, offense-free sovereign personal AI orchestrator (WARDEN kernel, signed memory spine, gated agent mesh).\"\nreadme = \"README.md\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.11\"\nlicense = { text = \"Proprietary\" }\nauthors = [{ name = \"SIGIL\" }]\nkeywords = [\"ai\", \"assistant\", \"local-first\", \"agents\", \"personal\", \"sovereign\"]\nclassifiers = [\n    \"Development Status :: 4 - Beta\",\n    \"Environment :: Console\",\n    \"Intended Audience :: End Users/Desktop\",\n    \"Operating System :: POSIX :: Linux\",\n    \"Programming Language :: Python :: 3 :: Only\",\n    \"Programming Language :: Python :: 3.11\",\n    \"Programming Language :: Python :: 3.12\",\n    \"Programming Language :: Python :: 3.13\",\n    \"Private :: Do Not Upload\",\n]\ndynamic = [\"version\"]\n\n# Core runtime dependencies — the memory loop, the MCP server, and the always-imported paths.\n# `numpy` is a genuine top-level import (sigil/voice/backends.py) and MUST be declared, not relied on\n# transitively via fastembed/onnxruntime.\ndependencies = [\n    \"cryptography==49.0.0\",\n    \"fastembed==0.8.0\",\n    \"kuzu==0.11.3\",\n    \"mcp==1.28.1\",\n    \"numpy>=1.26,<3\",\n    \"onnxruntime==1.27.0\",\n    \"pydantic==2.13.4\",\n    \"qdrant-client==1.18.0\",\n]\n\n# Optional stacks are lazy-imported and degrade gracefully; the extras make the install intentional\n# instead of trial-and-error. `pip install \"sigil[voice]\"` etc.\n[project.optional-dependencies]\nvoice = [\"sounddevice\", \"faster-whisper\", \"openwakeword\", \"piper-tts\"]\nsecrets = [\"keyring\"]\ncapture = [\"mss\"]                 # cross-platform screen capture (macOS/Windows backends)\ndev = [\"pytest>=8\", \"ruff>=0.6\", \"mypy>=1.10\"]\nall = [\"sounddevice\", \"faster-whisper\", \"openwakeword\", \"piper-tts\", \"keyring\", \"mss\"]\n\n# The `sigil` console command + `python -m sigil` both resolve to the CLI.\n[project.scripts]\nsigil = \"sigil.cli:main\"\n\n[tool.setuptools.dynamic]\nversion = { attr = \"sigil.__version__\" }\n\n[tool.setuptools.packages.find]\ninclude = [\"sigil*\"]\n\n# ── quality gates ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n[tool.ruff]\nline-length = 120\ntarget-version = \"py311\"\nexclude = [\"sigil/bridge/webapp\"]   # vendored/static front-end assets\n\n[tool.ruff.lint]\n# Pragmatic entry gate: block on REAL bug classes (pyflakes F: unused/undefined/f-strings; bugbear B:\n# likely bugs; E9: syntax/runtime errors) — NOT stylistic modernization (UP), import ordering (I), or\n# whitespace (E/W) on a large existing codebase. Tighten rule-by-rule over time.\nselect = [\"E9\", \"F\", \"B\"]\nignore = [\n    \"B008\",   # function call in an argument default — used deliberately for injectable defaults\n    \"B011\",   # assert False — used intentionally in tests/guards\n    \"B904\",   # raise ... from — style; tighten later (kept selected so B still catches real bug rules)\n    \"B905\",   # zip(strict=) — safety nudge; tighten later\n    \"B007\",   # unused loop control variable — style; tighten later\n]\n\n[tool.ruff.lint.per-file-ignores]\n\"tests/*\" = [\"E402\", \"F401\", \"F841\", \"B011\"]   # tests: late imports, unused re-exports/intermediates, assert False\n\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = [\"tests\"]\naddopts = \"-q\"\n\n[tool.mypy]\npython_version = \"3.13\"\n# Pragmatic entry posture for a large, dynamically-typed codebase: check what's annotated, don't block\n# on the untyped majority. Tighten module-by-module over time.\nignore_missing_imports = true\nwarn_unused_ignores = true\nwarn_redundant_casts = true\ncheck_untyped_defs = false\nexclude = [\"sigil/bridge/webapp\", \"tests\"]","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"f2b6f785-a7f9-4fa9-a204-1af002c12ec9","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:36:50.707Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d4e98156-49e9-422e-ada4-9aa6aa6fc594","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Ports the CRUCIBLE MCP posture (framework/v2/mcp/server.py): a fail-closed tool\nallowlist, no write path, and provenance on every result — each hit is a real, tamper-\nevident spine record (seq + entry_hash) so answers are CITED, and an empty result says so\nrather than inviting fabrication (SIGIL doctrine §1.5, prove-don't-guess).\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom mcp.server.fastmcp import FastMCP\n\nfrom ..config import EMBED_MODEL, QDRANT_URL\nfrom ..reuse import assert_no_offense\nfrom ..spine.checkpoint import verify_checkpoint\nfrom ..spine.store import SpineStore\nfrom ..vectors.index import VectorIndex\n\nassert_no_offense()  # doctrine §12: no engine module may be loaded in a SIGIL process\n\nmcp = FastMCP(\"sigil-memory\")\n_index: VectorIndex | None = None\n\n\ndef _idx() -> VectorIndex:\n    global _index\n    if _index is None:\n        _index = VectorIndex()\n    return _index\n\n\n@mcp.tool()\ndef memory_search(query: str, k: int = 8) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Search the owner's ENTIRE Claude history — everything they've built, decided, or\n    discussed — and return ranked memory records WITH PROVENANCE so you can cite them.\n\n    Each result is a real, tamper-evident spine record: `seq` + `entry_hash` uniquely\n    identify it, `when`/`session`/`project` locate it. When you answer from these, CITE\n    the seq(s). If `results` is empty, memory genuinely has nothing — say so; do NOT\n    fabricate an answer.\n\n    Args:\n        query: what to recall (natural language).\n        k: max results (default 8).\n    \"\"\"\n    hits, is_grounded = _idx().grounded(query, k=max(1, min(int(k), 25)))\n    if not is_grounded:\n        return {\"results\": [],\n                \"note\": \"No strongly-grounded match in SIGIL memory for this query. Do not fabricate — \"\n                        \"tell the owner memory has nothing solid on this (optionally suggest rephrasing).\"}\n    return {\n        \"results\": [\n            {\n                \"seq\": h[\"seq\"], \"kind\": h[\"kind\"], \"when\": h.get(\"ts\"),\n                \"session\": h.get(\"session_id\"), \"project\": h.get(\"project\"),\n                \"entry_hash\": h.get(\"entry_hash\"), \"score\": round(h[\"score\"], 4),\n                \"text\": h.get(\"text\"),\n            }\n\n===== README: install / mcp / qdrant / kernel mentions =====\n80:        Kùzu graph (entities)   Qdrant vectors            8 read-only, cited MCP tools\n85:(double-rebuild → identical). The MCP server, the cockpit, and the phone are read/act *surfaces* over it —\n101:  in a local Qdrant. Local vector store (Qdrant on loopback)\n181:docker run -d --name sigil-qdrant --restart unless-stopped -p 127.0.0.1:6333:6333 \\\n182:    -v ~/.sigil/vectors:/qdrant/storage qdrant/qdrant\n184:# 3.
