{"parentUuid":"a0a7df70-1608-4697-99c4-e7a3641087cc","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a08348e19783bf157","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdBGbmCizGvTwMNaKebfQ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_0123Lh7FwfCzD8VgL6noCk5S","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"/home/kali/sigil/README.md","offset":160,"limit":120},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3994,"cache_read_input_tokens":75191,"output_tokens":186,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3994},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":186,"cache_read_input_tokens":75191,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3994,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3994,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdBGbj4F5hZmnT7wCKk8B","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"2fdaf724-bda9-4d11-ab6f-8c6f3227abb3","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:37:03.785Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"2fdaf724-bda9-4d11-ab6f-8c6f3227abb3","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"6d6f6da1-0dda-49ba-98c2-ecb094db874a","agentId":"a08348e19783bf157","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0123Lh7FwfCzD8VgL6noCk5S","type":"tool_result","content":"160\t  ⌘K palette showing the predicted WARDEN tier before acting; and a CSRF-proof, owner-signed action\n161\t  plane (the private key never enters the browser).\n162\t- **Cross-platform + mobile** (`sigil/platform/`, `sigil/mesh/`, `sigil/bridge/`) — per-OS backends\n163\t  (Linux real; macOS/Windows/Android honest-degrading), OS-keyring secrets, an owner-signed\n164\t  device-authorization ledger, and the **phone companion** below.\n165\t\n166\t---\n167\t\n168\t## Setup\n169\t\n170\t**Prerequisites:** Linux (primary), Python 3.13, Rust (for the kernel), Docker (for Qdrant), and\n171\toptionally Ollama (local VLM), a WireGuard/Tailscale tunnel (phone companion), and `ydotool`/`xdotool`\n172\t(gesture input on Linux).\n173\t\n174\t```bash\n175\t# 1. Python environment + dependencies\n176\tpython3 -m venv ~/.sigil/venv\n177\t~/.sigil/venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt\n178\t#    (cryptography, fastembed, kuzu, mcp, onnxruntime, pydantic, qdrant-client)\n179\t\n180\t# 2. Local vector store (Qdrant on loopback)\n181\tdocker run -d --name sigil-qdrant --restart unless-stopped -p 127.0.0.1:6333:6333 \\\n182\t    -v ~/.sigil/vectors:/qdrant/storage qdrant/qdrant\n183\t\n184\t# 3. Build the Rust authorization kernel\n185\tcd kernel && cargo build --release && cd ..     # → kernel/target/release/sigil-kernel\n186\t\n187\t# 4. Build the memory loop\n188\tV=~/.sigil/venv/bin/python\n189\t$V -m sigil.cli ingest      # your Claude history + subagents (+ --git, --docs) → spine\n190\t$V -m sigil.cli index       # spine → vectors (incremental)\n191\t$V -m sigil.cli graph       # rebuild the deterministic entity graph\n192\t$V -m sigil.cli sign        # anchor the spine (Ed25519 signed head)\n193\t\n194\t# 5. Register the MCP memory server (Claude Code + Claude Desktop)\n195\t#    add to ~/.claude.json and ~/.config/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json under \"mcpServers\":\n196\t#    \"sigil-memory\": { \"command\": \"/home/<you>/.sigil/venv/bin/python\",\n197\t#                      \"args\": [\"-m\",\"sigil.mcp.server\"],\n198\t#                      \"env\": {\"SIGIL_HOME\": \"/home/<you>/.sigil\"} }\n199\t```\n200\t\n201\tRuntime data lives under `~/.sigil/` (spine, keys, vectors, graph — private, 0700). Code lives in this\n202\trepo. The Qdrant server URL is persisted in `~/.sigil/sigil.env`.\n203\t\n204\t---\n205\t\n206\t## Usage\n207\t\n208\t```bash\n209\tV=~/.sigil/venv/bin/python\n210\tS=\"$V -m sigil.cli\"\n211\t\n212\t# ── memory ───────────────────────────────────────────────\n213\t$S search \"the veracity firewall\"      # cited recall from the spine\n214\t$S consolidate --provider heuristic    # offline extraction (default, no frontier spend)\n215\t$S consolidate --provider claude       # agent-driven (headless claude -p on Max)\n216\t$S status                              # counts + live integrity\n217\t$S verify <report.json>                # re-verify a report's citations offline\n218\t\n219\t# ── the WARDEN authorization kernel ──────────────────────\n220\t$S warden status                       # kill-switch / promotion state\n221\t$S warden kill        /  $S warden release\n222\t$S warden promote <agent> / revoke <agent>\n223\t$S audit                               # the signed action log\n224\t\n225\t# ── agents ───────────────────────────────────────────────\n226\t$S agents brief                        # the unprompted morning brief (STEWARD)\n227\t$S agents triage                       # inbox triage (ENVOY drafts-only)\n228\t$S agents sentinel                     # salience / budget pass\n229\t$S agents research \"<question>\"        # sourced research (SCHOLAR / SCRIBE)\n230\t$S agents artifice                     # background coding (ARTIFICER → PR)\n231\t$S agents bastion                      # defensive posture scan (own infra only)\n232\t$S agents perceive --image <path>      # vision (OCR authoritative + local VLM advisory)\n233\t$S agents perceive --recall \"<X>\"      # \"where did I last see X?\"\n234\t\n235\t# ── voice & scraping ─────────────────────────────────────\n236\t$S voice --mic                         # full-duplex voice (--asr/--tts/--wake options)\n237\t$S scrape <seed-url>                   # grounded web research (robots-respected, cited)\n238\t\n239\t# ── the local cockpit ────────────────────────────────────\n240\t$S serve                               # loopback provenance-first web UI (prints a token)\n241\t$S dashboard                           # read-only TUI dashboard\n242\t$S host                                # this host's capability descriptor\n243\t```\n244\t\n245\tAny registered Claude session (Code or Desktop) can also just *ask* — e.g. *\"what did I decide about the\n246\tkill-switch\"* — and gets a cited answer via the MCP tools.\n247\t\n248\t---\n249\t\n250\t## The phone companion\n251\t\n252\tYour phone becomes a **signed remote-control + approval + gesture surface over WireGuard**. The engine\n253\tstays on your PC; the phone holds only its own Ed25519 device key — your owner trust-root never leaves the\n254\tdesktop, and the desktop **verifies** every request (it never signs on the phone's behalf).\n255\t\n256\t**Try it now, no phone or tunnel needed** — a runnable end-to-end demo against the real server on loopback:\n257\t\n258\t```bash\n259\t~/.sigil/venv/bin/python demo/companion_demo.py    # pair → approve → relay → recall → arm → panic\n260\t```\n261\t\n262\t**Real WireGuard / Tailscale** (full guide in [`demo/README.md`](demo/README.md)):\n263\t\n264\t```bash\n265\tsigil bridge serve --addr 10.13.13.1      # bind to the tunnel IP (refuses 0.0.0.0/public);\n266\t                                          # mints owner-pinned self-signed TLS + prints its fingerprint\n267\tsigil mesh authorize phone-1 <pubkey>     # pair the phone, confirming the fingerprint it shows\n268\tsigil mesh list-devices  /  sigil mesh revoke phone-1 <pubkey>\n269\t```\n270\t\n271\tThen, from the installable PWA the desktop serves, the phone can: **approve/deny** queued A2·A3 actions,\n272\t**panic-halt** (fail-safe; release stays owner-only), **relay** a command to the WARDEN-gated KERNEL,\n273\tbrowse a **read-only cockpit** + live push feed, **recall** grounded on-screen history, and (opt-in) **arm\n274\ta gesture session** and act as a **remote trackpad** — with every guarantee (A1 pointer only; type/launch\n275\talways queue; owner disarm/panic/revoke always wins) intact over the wire.\n276\t\n277\t---\n278\t\n279\t## Security model"}]},"uuid":"672b69bf-299b-46ff-a88e-9fd6893897ce","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:37:03.830Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"2fdaf724-bda9-4d11-ab6f-8c6f3227abb3","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"f4072af6-9ac5-4472-8d77-1630d211070b","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a08348e19783bf157","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdBGmNocahiUvUSwNzydW","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_013JjgVrwPveZyp1vL3TzFTD","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/kali/sigil/deploy/systemd/sigil-bridge@.service","content":"# SIGIL WireGuard phone-bridge — systemd USER service (template unit)\n# =====================================================================\n# Runs `sigil bridge serve --addr %i`, the WS-D phone transport. The\n# instance name (%i) IS the bind address, e.g.:\n#\n#     systemctl --user enable --now sigil-bridge@10.13.13.1\n#\n# ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n# │  PIN THE BIND ADDRESS TO A WIREGUARD / TAILSCALE IP — NEVER        │\n# │  0.0.0.0 AND NEVER A PUBLIC ADDRESS.                               │\n# │                                                                    │\n# │  The server itself fails closed (`bind_ok`): it binds ONLY         │\n# │  loopback, an RFC1918 private address, or the Tailscale CGNAT      │\n# │  range (100.64.0.0/10). A public / unspecified address is refused  │\n# │  with exit 2 and no cert is minted. The tunnel, not the transport, │\n# │  is the network boundary.                                          │\n# └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n#\n# INSTALL (user-level, no root):\n#   mkdir -p ~/.config/systemd/user\n#   cp deploy/systemd/sigil-bridge@.service ~/.config/systemd/user/\n#   cp deploy/bridge.env.example            ~/.sigil/bridge.env    # then edit\n#   systemctl --user daemon-reload\n#   systemctl --user enable --now sigil-bridge@10.13.13.1\n#   # keep it running after logout (optional): loginctl enable-linger \"$USER\"\n#\n# For an IPv6 tunnel address, escape it first:\n#   systemctl --user enable --now \"sigil-bridge@$(systemd-escape fd7a::1)\"\n#\n# Logs → the journal:  journalctl --user -u sigil-bridge@10.13.13.1 -f\n\n[Unit]\nDescription=SIGIL WireGuard phone bridge (bound to %i)\nDocumentation=file:%h/sigil/RUNBOOK.md\n# The bridge relays commands to the WARDEN-gated kernel and needs the tunnel up;\n# these are best-effort ordering hints, not hard requirements.\nAfter=network-online.target\nWants=network-online.target\n\n[Service]\nType=simple\n\n# The addr is the instance name (%i). SIGIL_HOME / port / log level / kernel\n# path come from the env file (optional: the leading '-' means a missing file\n# does not fail the unit; SIGIL defaults to ~/.sigil either way).\nEnvironmentFile=-%h/.sigil/bridge.env\nEnvironment=SIGIL_LOG_LEVEL=INFO\n\n# ExecStart uses the `sigil` console command installed into the project venv\n# (README canonical layout: python3 -m venv ~/.sigil/venv && ~/.sigil/venv/bin/pip\n# install -e .). If you did NOT `pip install -e .` (only requirements.txt), the\n# console script is absent — swap the line below for the `python -m sigil` form.\nWorkingDirectory=%h/.sigil\nExecStart=%h/.sigil/venv/bin/sigil bridge serve --addr %i --port ${SIGIL_BRIDGE_PORT}\n# Alternative if the `sigil` console script is not installed:\n# ExecStart=%h/.sigil/venv/bin/python -m sigil bridge serve --addr %i --port ${SIGIL_BRIDGE_PORT}\n\n# --- lifecycle -------------------------------------------------------------\nRestart=on-failure\nRestartSec=5\n# The server installs SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers and shuts down serve_forever\n# cleanly (see bridge/server.py:_serve_until_signal), so a plain SIGTERM is a\n# graceful stop, not an abrupt kill. Give it headroom to drain + close the\n# (TLS-wrapped) listening socket (it joins its worker with a 5s timeout).\nKillSignal=SIGTERM\nTimeoutStopSec=20\n\n# --- hardening (verified NOT to break the daemon) --------------------------\n# Least privilege without cutting off what the bridge needs (loopback+tunnel\n# network, read of the repo/venv, write of ~/.sigil).\nNoNewPrivileges=yes\nProtectSystem=strict\n# ~/.sigil is the ONLY path the process writes (spine append, TLS cert cache,\n# receipts). strict makes everything else read-only; this re-grants that tree.\nReadWritePaths=%h/.sigil\nPrivateTmp=yes\nProtectKernelTunables=yes\nProtectKernelModules=yes\nProtectControlGroups=yes\nRestrictSUIDSGID=yes\nLockPersonality=yes\n# NOTE — deliberately NOT set, because they WOULD break this daemon:\n#   PrivateNetwork=yes  → hides the WireGuard interface; the bind would fail.\n#   ProtectHome=yes     → hides ~/.sigil (spine + keys); everything would fail.\n#   MemoryDenyWriteExecute=yes → some bundled native libs dislike W^X; left off.\n\n[Install]\nWantedBy=default.target\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":8940,"cache_read_input_tokens":81914,"output_tokens":1906,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":8940},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":1906,"cache_read_input_tokens":81914,"cache_creation_input_tokens":8940,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":8940,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdBGmMNo25t3Yhby66MVN","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"80826d60-0c74-4d49-b300-3f6fd3174876","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:39:36.881Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Triggered by sigil-consolidate.timer; not\n# meant to be started by hand except for a manual run:\n#\n#     systemctl --user start sigil-consolidate.service\n#     journalctl --user -u sigil-consolidate -e\n#\n# INSTALL (paired with the timer):\n#   cp deploy/systemd/sigil-consolidate.{service,timer} ~/.config/systemd/user/\n#   cp deploy/bridge.env.example ~/.sigil/bridge.env    # shared env (if not already)\n#   systemctl --user daemon-reload\n#   systemctl --user enable --now sigil-consolidate.timer\n\n[Unit]\nDescription=SIGIL nightly memory consolidation (heuristic, offline, no spend)\nDocumentation=file:%h/sigil/RUNBOOK.md\n\n[Service]\nType=oneshot\n\nEnvironmentFile=-%h/.sigil/bridge.env\nEnvironment=SIGIL_LOG_LEVEL=INFO\n\nWorkingDirectory=%h/.sigil\nExecStart=%h/.sigil/venv/bin/sigil consolidate --provider heuristic\n# Alternative if the `sigil` console script is not installed:\n# ExecStart=%h/.sigil/venv/bin/python -m sigil consolidate --provider heuristic\n\n# --- hardening (same posture as the bridge) --------------------------------\n# It appends promoted memories to the spine and re-signs the head, so it needs\n# write access to ~/.sigil; nothing else.\nNoNewPrivileges=yes\nProtectSystem=strict\nReadWritePaths=%h/.sigil\nPrivateTmp=yes\nProtectKernelTunables=yes\nProtectKernelModules=yes\nProtectControlGroups=yes\nRestrictSUIDSGID=yes\nLockPersonality=yes\n# NOT set (would break it): ProtectHome (hides the spine), PrivateNetwork\n# (harmless here but unnecessary), MemoryDenyWriteExecute (native-lib safety).\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1963,"cache_read_input_tokens":90854,"output_tokens":873,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1963},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":873,"cache_read_input_tokens":90854,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1963,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1963,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdBGpBMNMwR8o23FbDgqh","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"ad663e39-c7c4-42d7-aab4-5b4e62378d09","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:40:34.531Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

confirm next run\n\n[Unit]\nDescription=Nightly trigger for SIGIL memory consolidation\nDocumentation=file:%h/sigil/RUNBOOK.md\n\n[Timer]\n# 03:17 local time daily — a low-traffic hour, with a minute offset so it does\n# not stampede with every other 03:00 cron on the box.\nOnCalendar=*-*-* 03:17:00\n# If the machine was asleep/off at 03:17, run once on the next wake instead of\n# skipping the night entirely.\nPersistent=true\n# Smear the start by up to 15 min so many hosts do not fire simultaneously.\nRandomizedDelaySec=900\nUnit=sigil-consolidate.service\n\n[Install]\nWantedBy=timers.target\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":930,"cache_read_input_tokens":92817,"output_tokens":440,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":930},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":440,"cache_read_input_tokens":92817,"cache_creation_input_tokens":930,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":930,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdBGu54z1QW7M9v6NoWaE","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"8632e882-2e2b-4fa8-9a42-e00a75e6c965","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:41:02.295Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"480a90c6-5645-48b9-8231-4d50e68bad11","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a08348e19783bf157","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdBGuz1Ac9vsQheBuVqz4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_018ZgkAkXjswP28zxr7ykeYz","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/kali/sigil/deploy/logrotate/sigil","content":"# SIGIL log rotation — TEMPLATE, only needed if you redirect logs to a file.\n# =====================================================================\n# By default SIGIL logs to STDERR (sigil/obs.py:configure_logging). Under the\n# systemd units in this directory that stderr is captured by the JOURNAL, and\n# journald already rotates/vacuums it — you do NOT need this file. Cap journal\n# growth instead with, e.g.:\n#     journalctl --user --vacuum-size=200M\n#     # or set SystemMaxUse= / MaxRetentionSec= in /etc/systemd/journald.conf\n#\n# THIS FILE is for the other case: you run SIGIL outside systemd (or add\n# `--append` style redirection) and send stderr to a real file, e.g.\n#     sigil bridge serve --addr 10.13.13.1 2>> ~/.sigil/logs/bridge.log\n#\n# Because SIGIL holds the log file descriptor open for the life of the process\n# and does not reopen on SIGHUP, use `copytruncate` (rotate a copy, then\n# truncate the live file in place) rather than `create` — otherwise the daemon\n# would keep writing to the rotated-away inode.\n#\n# USAGE\n#   System-wide (needs root): drop this at /etc/logrotate.d/sigil and edit the\n#   glob to the operator's real home (logrotate.d does not expand ~ or $HOME).\n#   Per-user (no root): run logrotate yourself with a private state file, e.g.\n#   from a user cron/timer:\n#       logrotate -s ~/.sigil/logrotate.state ~/sigil/deploy/logrotate/sigil\n\n/home/*/.sigil/logs/*.log {\n    weekly\n    rotate 8\n    size 50M\n    compress\n    delaycompress\n    copytruncate\n    missingok\n    notifempty\n Anything you set here also overrides SIGIL's built-in defaults.\n\n# --- data root (defaults to ~/.sigil; only set to relocate the spine) -------\n# SIGIL_HOME=/home/youruser/.sigil\n\n# --- bridge bind port (the ADDRESS is the systemd instance name, e.g.\n#     sigil-bridge@10.13.13.1 → --addr 10.13.13.1). Leave unset for embedded/local mode. --------\n# SIGIL_QDRANT_URL=http://127.0.0.1:6333\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":789,"cache_read_input_tokens":94368,"output_tokens":810,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":789},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":810,"cache_read_input_tokens":94368,"cache_creation_input_tokens":789,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":789,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdBGwGtCFPUCKpeAhDj3D","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"de234d25-b521-42aa-962f-15535935d94e","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:41:36.480Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"0a118a28-9ae0-429b-8c5f-f005a677f1bf","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a08348e19783bf157","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdBGxyFc95Kd4JeDLJfDD","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_019RFTBvronBnxRYqoGMFFfb","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/kali/sigil/RUNBOOK.md","content":"# SIGIL Operator Runbook\n\nProduction operations for a SIGIL install: run the daemons, self-check,\n**back up the irreplaceable spine**, upgrade, and troubleshoot.\n\nSIGIL is local-first and owner-only. All runtime state lives under\n`~/.sigil/` (mode `0700`); the code lives in this repo. Nothing here reaches\nthe network except the loopback Qdrant container and, if you enable it, the\nWireGuard-bound phone bridge (which binds a private/tunnel IP only — never a\npublic address).\n\nDeployment assets referenced below live in [`deploy/`](deploy/).\n\n---\n\n## 1. Install\n\nFull walk-through with prerequisites is in the [README](README.md#setup)\n(Linux, Python 3.13, Rust for the kernel, Docker for Qdrant). Summary:\n\n```bash\n# 1. venv + dependencies (README canonical layout: venv under ~/.sigil)\npython3 -m venv ~/.sigil/venv\n~/.sigil/venv/bin/pip install -e .          # installs the `sigil` console command\n#   add optional stacks as needed:  pip install -e \".[all]\"   (voice, secrets, capture)\n#   or the minimal runtime only:    pip install -r requirements.txt   (no console script)\n\n# 2. Local vector store (Qdrant on loopback)\ndocker run -d --name sigil-qdrant --restart unless-stopped -p 127.0.0.1:6333:6333 \\\n    -v ~/.sigil/vectors:/qdrant/storage qdrant/qdrant\n\n# 3. Build the Rust authorization kernel\ncd kernel && cargo build --release && cd ..     # → kernel/target/release/sigil-kernel\n\n# 4. Build the memory loop\nsigil ingest        # Claude history + subagents (+ --git --docs) → spine\nsigil index         # spine → vectors (incremental)\nsigil graph         # rebuild the deterministic entity graph\nsigil sign          # anchor the spine (Ed25519 signed head)\n\n# 5. Register the MCP memory server (read-only, cited recall) with Claude\n#    add to ~/.claude.json and ~/.config/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json under \"mcpServers\":\n#    \"sigil-memory\": { \"command\": \"/home/<you>/.sigil/venv/bin/python\",\n#                      \"args\": [\"-m\",\"sigil.mcp.server\"],\n#                      \"env\": {\"SIGIL_HOME\": \"/home/<you>/.sigil\"} }\n\n# 6. Verify the install\nsigil doctor\n```\n\n> `sigil <cmd>`, `~/.sigil/venv/bin/sigil <cmd>`, and\n> `~/.sigil/venv/bin/python -m sigil <cmd>` are equivalent. The systemd units\n> use an absolute venv path so they do not depend on a login `PATH`.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Run the daemons\n\nSIGIL has two long-lived surfaces. Ship both as **user-level** systemd units\n(no root) from [`deploy/systemd/`](deploy/systemd/).\n\n### 2a. Phone bridge (`sigil-bridge@.service`)\n\nServes the WireGuard-bound phone transport. **The instance name is the bind\naddress** — always a WireGuard/Tailscale/loopback IP, never `0.0.0.0` and\nnever public (the server fails closed on anything else).\n\n```bash\nmkdir -p ~/.config/systemd/user\ncp deploy/systemd/sigil-bridge@.service ~/.config/systemd/user/\ncp deploy/bridge.env.example            ~/.sigil/bridge.env    # then edit\nchmod 600 ~/.sigil/bridge.env\nsystemctl --user daemon-reload\n\nsystemctl --user enable --now sigil-bridge@10.13.13.1   # 10.13.13.1 = your WG IP\nloginctl enable-linger \"$USER\"        # optional: keep running after logout\n```\n\nManage / observe:\n\n```bash\nsystemctl --user status  sigil-bridge@10.13.13.1\nsystemctl --user stop    sigil-bridge@10.13.13.1     # graceful SIGTERM (drains + closes socket)\nsystemctl --user restart sigil-bridge@10.13.13.1\njournalctl  --user -u    sigil-bridge@10.13.13.1 -f  # follow logs\njournalctl  --user -u 'sigil-bridge@*' -e            # all bridge instances\n```\n\nOn first start the bridge prints its **self-signed TLS fingerprint** — pin it\nonce on the phone; it is stable across restarts, so a later change means MITM.\nPair a phone (owner, at the desktop):\n\n```bash\nsigil mesh authorize phone-1 <device-pubkey>   # confirm the fingerprint the phone shows\nsigil mesh list-devices\nsigil mesh revoke phone-1 <device-pubkey>      # takes effect on the next request\n```\n\n> IPv6 tunnel address? Escape it: `sigil-bridge@$(systemd-escape fd7a::1)`.\n\n### 2b. Nightly consolidation (`sigil-consolidate.timer`)\n\nRuns `sigil consolidate --provider heuristic` off-peak — **offline, no\nfrontier/API spend**. Oneshot service driven by a timer.\n\n```bash\ncp deploy/systemd/sigil-consolidate.{service,timer} ~/.config/systemd/user/\nsystemctl --user daemon-reload\nsystemctl --user enable --now sigil-consolidate.timer\nsystemctl --user list-timers 'sigil-*'               # confirm next run\nsystemctl --user start sigil-consolidate.service     # run once, now\n```\n\n### 2c. MCP memory server\n\nNot a daemon you run — Claude Code / Claude Desktop spawn it on demand via the\nregistration in step 5 above (stdio, read-only, loopback). Nothing to\nenable/start; if recall stops working, re-check the JSON registration and that\nthe `command`/`env` paths are correct, then restart the Claude client.\n\n### 2d. Local cockpit (optional, on demand)\n\n`sigil serve` starts a loopback-only web UI and prints a one-time token; it is\ninteractive, not a managed service. Run it when you want the cockpit.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Self-check — `sigil doctor`\n\n`sigil doctor` prints one line per check plus the effective config (secrets\nredacted) and exits non-zero if any check fails.\n\n| Check | Means | If it fails |\n|-------|-------|-------------|\n| `sigil_home_writable` | `~/.sigil` exists and is writable | fix ownership/permissions: `chmod 700 ~/.sigil` (see §4 for the full mode map) |\n| `kernel_binary` | the Rust WARDEN kernel was found | build it (`cd kernel && cargo build --release`) or set `SIGIL_KERNEL_BIN` to its path |\n| `claude_cli` | the `claude` CLI is on PATH | only needed for LLM-backed consolidate/agents; install it or set `SIGIL_CLAUDE_BIN`. Harmless if you only run the heuristic provider |\n| `qdrant` | vector store reachable | embedded mode always passes; server mode probes `SIGIL_QDRANT_URL/readyz` — start the container (`docker start sigil-qdrant`) |\n| `keyring` | an OS keyring backend is present | optional — without it, secrets fall back to `~/.sigil/sigil.env` (`0600`). Install the `secrets` extra for a real keyring |\n\nRun it after every install, upgrade, and reboot.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Backup & restore — CRITICAL\n\n**The spine and the owner keys are irreplaceable.** The spine is an\nappend-only, hash-chained ledger of everything SIGIL knows; the owner Ed25519\nkey is the trust root that signs the head and every approval. Lose the key and\nyou **permanently lose the ability to sign approvals or re-anchor the head** —\nexisting signatures still verify, but you can never produce new ones.\n\n### What to back up (irreplaceable)\n\nEverything under **`~/.sigil/spine/`**:\n\n| Path | What it is |\n|------|-----------|\n| `~/.sigil/spine/spine.jsonl` | the append-only, hash-chained event ledger |\n| `~/.sigil/spine/head.json` | the Ed25519-signed head anchor (anti-truncation) |\n| `~/.sigil/spine/keys/` | the owner + WARDEN keypairs (**the trust root**) |\n\nAlso worth capturing (convenient, but **rebuildable** — see below):\n`~/.sigil/sigil.env` (config + secrets fallback), `~/.sigil/bridge/` (pinned\nTLS cert), `~/.sigil/qdrant/` (vectors), `~/.sigil/graph/` (entity graph).\nVectors and the graph are *deterministic projections of the spine* — after a\nrestore you can regenerate them with `sigil index` and `sigil graph` instead of\nrestoring them. The spine + keys you cannot regenerate.\n\nThe simplest correct backup is a copy of the **whole `~/.sigil/` tree**.\n\n### How to back up\n\nThe spine is append-only and hash-chained, so a plain file copy taken while the\nprocess is quiescent is a consistent snapshot — no database dump needed.\n\n```bash\n# Stop writers first (bridge + timer) so the snapshot is clean:\nsystemctl --user stop 'sigil-bridge@*' sigil-consolidate.timer\n\n# Snapshot everything (preserve permissions with -p / --numeric-ids):\ntar -C \"$HOME\" -cpzf ~/sigil-backup-$(date +%F).tar.gz .sigil\n#   or incrementally:\nrsync -aH --delete ~/.sigil/ /path/to/backup/sigil/\n\n# Resume:\nsystemctl --user start 'sigil-bridge@*' sigil-consolidate.timer\n```\n\nA snapshot taken while writers are running is *usually* fine (append-only means\na torn write can only be a truncated tail, which reads self-recover — see §6),\nbut stopping writers guarantees a clean point-in-time copy.\n\n### How to restore\n\n```bash\ntar -C \"$HOME\" -xpzf ~/sigil-backup-YYYY-MM-DD.tar.gz     # restores ~/.sigil/\n\n# Re-assert owner-only permissions (a restore can widen them):\nchmod 700 ~/.sigil ~/.sigil/spine ~/.sigil/spine/keys\nchmod 600 ~/.sigil/spine/keys/*  ~/.sigil/sigil.env 2>/dev/null || true\n\n# Validate integrity BEFORE trusting the restore:\nsigil verify        # chain + signed head; exits non-zero on any break\nsigil status        # record counts + live integrity\n\n# Regenerate the projections if you did not restore them:\nsigil index && sigil graph\n```\n\n`sigil verify` re-walks the hash chain and checks the signed head; a clean\n`chain OK` / `head OK` confirms the restore is intact and untampered.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Upgrade\n\n```bash\ngit -C ~/sigil pull                       # (run git yourself; the runbook does not)\ncd ~/sigil\n~/.sigil/venv/bin/pip install -e .        # or -e \".[all]\"\ncd kernel && cargo build --release && cd ..   # rebuild the kernel if it changed\nsigil doctor                              # re-check the install\n~/.sigil/venv/bin/pytest -q               # run the test suite before trusting the upgrade\nsystemctl --user restart 'sigil-bridge@*' # pick up the new code\n```\n\nBack up (§4) before a major upgrade. The spine format is append-only and\nforward-compatible; an upgrade never rewrites existing records.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Troubleshooting\n\n| Symptom | Check | Fix |\n|---------|-------|-----|\n| Bridge exits immediately / \"refusing to bind\" | the `--addr` (instance name) is public or `0.0.0.0` | bind a WireGuard/Tailscale/loopback IP: `sigil-bridge@10.13.13.1`. `bind_ok` allows loopback, RFC1918 private, and Tailscale CGNAT (`100.64.0.0/10`) only |\n| `doctor`: `kernel_binary` not found / relay fails | the Rust kernel is unbuilt or off-PATH | `cd kernel && cargo build --release`, or set `SIGIL_KERNEL_BIN=/…/sigil-kernel` in `~/.sigil/bridge.env` |\n| `doctor`: `qdrant` unreachable (server mode) | the Qdrant container is down | `docker start sigil-qdrant` (or unset `SIGIL_QDRANT_URL` to use embedded mode) |\n| `search`/recall returns nothing | vectors not indexed after new ingest | `sigil index`; confirm with `sigil status` (`last_indexed_seq`) |\n| `verify` / `status` reports a torn spine tail | a write was interrupted (power loss mid-append) | reads self-recover past a torn trailing line; re-run `sigil verify` — a clean `chain OK` confirms recovery. If the chain still fails mid-file, restore from backup (§4) |\n| `chain FAIL` mid-file (not the tail) | the ledger was edited/corrupted | do NOT append further; restore `~/.sigil/spine/` from the last good backup and `sigil verify` |\n| Phone can't pair / requests rejected | device not authorized, or TLS fingerprint mismatch | re-run `sigil mesh authorize <id> <pubkey>` confirming the fingerprint the phone shows; check `sigil mesh list-devices`; a changed TLS fingerprint means MITM — investigate, do not blindly re-pin |\n| Bridge denies every request (403/401) | anti-rebind Host/Origin gate, or a revoked device | reach it via the exact bound `IP:PORT` (not a hostname); confirm the device is still in `list-devices` |\n| `keyring` check fails | no OS keyring backend | optional — secrets fall back to `~/.sigil/sigil.env` (`0600`); install the `secrets` extra for a real backend |\n| Unit won't start under systemd | wrong `ExecStart` path | ensure the venv `sigil` exists (`pip install -e .`), or switch the unit's `ExecStart` to the `python -m sigil` alternative line |\n\n---\n\n## 7. Log levels & observability\n\n- **Where logs go.** SIGIL logs to **stderr** (`sigil/obs.py`). Under the\n  systemd units that stderr is captured by **journald** — use\n  `journalctl --user -u sigil-bridge@<ip>`. journald handles rotation; cap it\n  with `journalctl --user --vacuum-size=200M` or `journald.conf`. Only if you\n  redirect stderr to a file (e.g. `~/.sigil/logs/*.log`) do you need the\n  [`deploy/logrotate/sigil`](deploy/logrotate/sigil) template.\n- **Verbosity.** Set `SIGIL_LOG_LEVEL` (`DEBUG`|`INFO`|`WARNING`|`ERROR`) — in\n  `~/.sigil/bridge.env` for the units, or the environment for ad-hoc runs.\n  Default `INFO`. An unknown value falls back to `INFO` rather than erroring.\n- **What SIGIL logs on the bridge.** Accepted effectful actions at `INFO`,\n  denials at `WARNING` — enough to answer \"who did what\" over the tunnel.\n- **What is NEVER logged.** Secrets, tokens, API keys, device signatures,\n  request bodies, relayed command text, or full public keys. Done.\n\n## Summary\n\nAdded the operational/deployment layer for SIGIL as **new files only** (no existing `sigil/`, `pyproject.toml`, or `.github/` code touched), **uncommitted**, and **no git commands run**. `git diff --name-only` is empty; `git status` shows only untracked `RUNBOOK.md` and `deploy/`.\n\n### Files created\n- `/home/kali/sigil/RUNBOOK.md` — operator runbook: Install, Run the daemons (bridge + consolidate timer + MCP + cockpit), `sigil doctor` self-check table, **Backup & restore** (the critical section), Upgrade, a symptom→check→fix Troubleshooting table, and Log levels & observability.\n- `/home/kali/sigil/deploy/systemd/sigil-bridge@.service` — templated user unit running `sigil bridge serve --addr %i`, with `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=5`, `KillSignal=SIGTERM`, `TimeoutStopSec=20`, `EnvironmentFile=-%h/.sigil/bridge.env`, `Environment=SIGIL_LOG_LEVEL=INFO`, and the requested hardening (`NoNewPrivileges`, `ProtectSystem=strict`, `ReadWritePaths=%h/.sigil`, `PrivateTmp`) plus a few always-safe extras. Header pins the bind to a WireGuard IP, never 0.0.0.0.\n- `/home/kali/sigil/deploy/systemd/sigil-consolidate.service` + `.timer` — oneshot `sigil consolidate --provider heuristic` (offline, no spend) at `03:17` daily, `Persistent=true`, `RandomizedDelaySec=900`, same hardening + EnvironmentFile.\n- `/home/kali/sigil/deploy/logrotate/sigil` — logrotate template (weekly, rotate 8, size 50M, compress, `copytruncate`), documenting that SIGIL logs to stderr → journald under systemd so this file is only for the redirect-to-file case.\n- `/home/kali/sigil/deploy/bridge.env.example` — the systemd `EnvironmentFile` template (copy to `~/.sigil/bridge.env`).\n\n### Accuracy notes verified against source\n- Console command is `sigil` (`[project.scripts]`), installed by `pip install -e .`; ExecStart uses the venv-absolute `%h/.sigil/venv/bin/sigil` with a documented `python -m sigil` fallback. All `sigil <subcommand>` references were grep-checked against `cli.py`.\n- Bridge SIGTERM/SIGINT graceful shutdown confirmed in `bridge/server.py:_serve_until_signal`; `bind_ok` (loopback/RFC1918/Tailscale-CGNAT only, refuses public/0.0.0.0) and CLI default port 8734 confirmed.\n- `doctor` check names (`sigil_home_writable`, `kernel_binary`, `claude_cli`, `qdrant`, `keyring`) taken verbatim from `config.py`.\n\n### Key assumptions / decisions (flagging for the operator)\n1. **Corrected backup paths.** The task named `~/.sigil/keys/` and `~/.sigil/head.json`, but per `config.py` the real layout is `~/.sigil/spine/spine.jsonl`, `~/.sigil/spine/head.json`, and `~/.sigil/spine/keys/` (head and keys live **under** `spine/`). The runbook uses the correct paths and notes that vectors (`~/.sigil/qdrant`) and graph (`~/.sigil/graph`) are rebuildable projections (`sigil index`/`sigil graph`), so only `~/.sigil/spine/` is truly irreplaceable — the whole-tree tar is the simple correct default.\n2. **Template unit name.** I named the bridge unit `sigil-bridge@.service` (not `sigil-bridge.service`) because `%i` and the runbook's own `systemctl --user enable --now sigil-bridge@<wg-ip>` example require a template unit. `journalctl` examples use the instance/glob forms accordingly.\n3. **Addr source.** The bind address comes from the systemd instance name (`%i`); `bridge.env` carries `SIGIL_HOME`/port/log-level/kernel-path. `EnvironmentFile` uses a leading `-` (optional) so a missing file doesn't fail the unit — SIGIL defaults to `~/.sigil` regardless. Documented in the file.\n4. **Hardening kept conservative.** Added only directives that don't break a userspace network daemon, and explicitly commented-out `PrivateNetwork`, `ProtectHome`, and `MemoryDenyWriteExecute` with the reason each would break SIGIL.\n5. **`sigil verify` takes no argument** in the actual CLI (verifies the spine chain + signed head); the runbook uses that form, not the README's stale `verify <report.json>`.\n\n`systemd-analyze verify --user` passes clean on all three units."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1355,"cache_read_input_tokens":103396,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1355,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdBH882CZkScJ8QF3ZHow","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"eb62c891-7c12-4391-9005-03c1b6286db4","timestamp":"2026-07-19T10:44:14.996Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
